**Miyoshi, Manabu** D. Sc., Mem. of Imp. Academy, Emer. Prof. Imp. Tokyo Univ. and Dir. of its Botanical Garden till '23; b. 1861 in Gifu; grad. from Botany at the Tokyo Imp. Univ., class '90; next studied in Germany '91-95. Add. Hongo Nishikata-machi, Tokyo. — p. 63 ([scan leaf 858](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n858/mode/1up))

**Mizumachi, Kesaroku** Pres. of Board of Audit since '24; b. 1863 in Saga; grad. Law, Imp. Univ. Tokyo in '89; Junior Councillor of the Treasury '91; despatched to Europe to investigate financial and economic situation in '98; filled chair of civil code at the alma mater as add. post till '01; Dir. Finance Bureau '04; Vice-Minister of Finance in '06; Govt. Financial Agent at London '06-11; Vice-Governor of Bank of Japan '12-19. Add. Nagatacho, Tokyo. — p. 63 ([scan leaf 858](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n858/mode/1up))

**Mizuno, Kotoku** Captain, I.J.N. (retired '21); born 1877 at Matsuyama; grad. from the Naval Academy, in '99; Sub.-Lieute- nant in '00; took part in the Japan-Russia war of '04-05 in the capacity of Captain as a torpedo-boat Commander; travelled in Europe in '16 for inspection of the post-war military affairs and again in '19 for a similar purpose; retired from the active service in '21 at his own request and has since taken to writing as a warm advocate of peace and disarmament movement. Add. Komazawa, near Tokyo. — p. 63 ([scan leaf 858](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n858/mode/1up))

**Mizuno, Naoshi** Visc., Mem. House of Peers; b. 1879, a son of a scion of the Mizuno family, formerly a daimyo of the Yuki clan, Shimosa, by whom he was adopted; grad. from Law Imp. Tokyo Univ., '03; was Parl. Vice-Minister of War in the Wakatsuki Ministry '26-27. Add. Otsuka Kubocho, Tokyo. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 859](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n859/mode/1up))

**Mizuno, Rentaro** D.C.L., Minister of Education since Apr. '27, Crown Mem. House of Peers; b. 1868 in Akita; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., in '92; entered Home Office '95; sent abroad '96 to investigate copy-right affairs and attended as Japanese delegate at World's Conference for the Protection of Right of Industrial Property held at Brussels; framed the draft of the Copyright Law; Dir. of Public Works Bureau '10-12; Vice-Minister of Home Office '13-14; full Minister '16-'18; Civil Administrator, Korea, '20-22. Home Minister, '19-23 Add. Shiba Shirokane-Sarumachi, Tokyo. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 859](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n859/mode/1up))

**Mizuno, Toshinojo** D. Sc., Prof. at the Science Dep't, Kyoto Imp. Univ.; b. 1862 in Fukuoka-ken; grad. from the Science Coll. of the Tokyo Imp. Univ., '90; studied physics and electrical science in Germany, '99-01; appointed Assist.-Prof. at the Coll. of Science and Engineering of the Kyoto Imp. Univ., '98; full Prof. in '02. Add. Sakuragi-cho, Kamikyo-ku, Kyoto. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 859](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n859/mode/1up))

**Mizushima, Tetsuya** ex-Dir. of the Kobe Higher Com. School '03-25; b. 1864 in Oita-ken; grad. from the Tokyo Higher Commercial School, class '87; teacher at the Osaka Com. School, '88-89; in the service of the Fujita-gumi, Osaka, '89-91; next of the Yokohama Specie Bank, serving for three years at the New York Branch; Prof. at his alma mater, '96-03. Toured abroad on official mission. Add. Kobe. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 859](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n859/mode/1up))

**Mochizuki Keisuke** M.P. (Seiyukai), Minister of Communications since April '27, mine-owner in Hiroshima; b. Mar. 1867 in Hiroshima Pref.; was returned to the House of Representatives eight times from his native place; is one of the leading members of the Seiyukai party. Add. Shiba Kurumacho, Tokyo. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 859](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n859/mode/1up))

**Mogami, Kunizo** Dir. Yokohama Specie Bank, since '22; b. in Hyogo-ken, 1875; grad. from Tokyo Higher Commercial Sch. in 1897; its agent at New York, Dairen, Shanghai, London, Tientsin, etc. and Manager of the Tokyo Branch till '22. Add. Yokohama Specie Bank. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 859](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n859/mode/1up))

**Monobe, Nagaho** D. Eng., Engineer to the Home Office, Dir. of the Civil Engineering Experiment Station (Home Office), Professor of Hydrodynamics at the Tokyo Imp. Univ., Mem. of Seismological Research Institute and also of the Scientific Research Council, etc.; b. 1886 in Akita-ken; grad. from Civil Eng., Imp. Tokyo Univ., class '11; has devoted himself to river work since then and has designed improvement plan for a number of unruly rivers in the Main Island, besides supervising the technical side of bridge-work and investigating earthquake-proof work with special reference to civil engineering. Has written a number of theses on the theory of stability of embankments, earthquake proof engineering works, etc. Was awarded the Imp. Academy prize in '25. Add. Home Office. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 859](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n859/mode/1up))

**Mori, Benjiro** Pres. of the Nisshin Kisen (Japan-China S.S.) Co. Ltd. since '24; b. 1867 in Shinano. Edu. at Waseda, class '88, and further at the Electric course, Imp. Tokyo Univ. Entered the Japan Times in '95; served in the Nippon Yusen Kaisha, 1899-21 during which he was appointed Local Manager at Tientsin, Hongkong and Osaka; Mng.-Dir. of the present Co., '21-24. Add. Shimo-Shibuya, Tokyo. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 860](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n860/mode/1up))

**Mori, Hirozo** former Pres. of the Bank of Formosa; b. in 1878 in Tottori-ken; grad. from Tokyo Higher Commercial Sch. in '97; London agent, Yokohama Specie Bank; Manager of Kobe Branch and then Gen-Mng. of the Head Office; appointed Vice-Pres. of the Bank '23 and next its President till '27. Add. Taihoku. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 860](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n860/mode/1up))

**Mori, Gaisaburo** Principal of the 3rd High School; b. 1866 in Ishikawa-ken; grad. from the Coll. of Science (mathematics) of the Tokyo Imp. Univ., '11; Lecturer of the Tokyo Higher Commercial School; taught at the Miye Middle School, '83; Prof. at the Third High School, '95; Prof. at the Peers' School, '09; studied in Germany, England and America, '09-11; Principal of the Kyoto 1st Middle School '11; the present post since 1922. Add. Yoshida Hommachi, Kyoto. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 860](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n860/mode/1up))

**Mori, Kongo** Crown Mem. House of Peers nom. in '27, former Govt. Financial Agent at London, Paris and Washington, b. 1876 in Saga-ken; grad. from Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ. '00; secretary of the Financial Dept, '00; the London Agent post '13-26. Add. Koj. Shimo-Nibancho, Tokyo. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 860](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n860/mode/1up))

**Mori, Moto-aki** Prince, the House of the former Daimyo of Choshu that was chiefly instrumental in overthrowing the Tokugawa Regency and in consummating the Restoration. The present head was b. in 1865. Add. Takanawa, Tokyo. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 860](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n860/mode/1up))

**Mori, Shunrokuro** Dir. of the South Manchuria Railway Co., Ltd.; b. 1878 in Fukushima-ken; grad. from the Law Coll. of the Tokyo Imp. Univ., '02; passed the Higher Civil Service Examination, '02; entered the Finance Dep't as Secretary, '07; Dir. of the Banking Bureau of the same dep't in '16. Add. Yamashirocho, Dairen City. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 860](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n860/mode/1up))

**Mori, Yasusaburo** Minister to Chile and Bolivia; b. 1880 in Fukuoka-ken; edu. at Imp. Tokyo Univ. in Law, class '07 and passed the Dip. and Con. Serv. Exam. next year; Eleve-Con. at Amoy, '09; at Honolulu, '10; Consul at Lima, '12; at Antung, '16; Con.-Gen. at Tsinan, '19 with additional duty as Sec. to the Administration Dept. of the Tsingtao Army of Occupation; later transferred to Tsingtao; Emb. Counsellor at Rome '23 to '27; present post Dec. '27. Add. Jap. Legation, Chili. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 860](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n860/mode/1up))

**Morimoto, Kokichi** D.L., Ph. D. (Johns Hopkins), Prof. at Hokkaido Imp. Univ. from which he was graduated in '01; b. 1877 at Maizuru; specialized in Economy, Politics and History at the Baltimore University; engaged in university extension lectures at Chicago and Boston; is an authority on social problems and is manager of the Tokyo Cultural Life Society which is building the first apartment building in Tokyo. Works; Pro- blems of Living, New Efficiency of Problems of Living, Investigation of Middle Class, Investigation of Standard of Living (in English) etc. Add. Sapporo. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 860](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n860/mode/1up))

**Morimura, Kaisaku** Baron (2nd), Pres. Morimura Bank, Morimura Firm, Dir. Y'hama Specie Bank, Chairman of Japan Overseas Trade Association, etc.; b. 1873 in Tokyo, 2nd son of the late Ichizaemon Morimura, pioneer Christian and businessman; studied at Keio and further in U.S.A. for years. Add. Shiba Takanawa-Minami-cho, Tokyo. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 861](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n861/mode/1up))

**Morito, Tatsuo** member of the Ohara Social Research Institute at Osaka; b. '85 in Hiroshima-ken, edu. at Imp. Tokyo Univ. in Law, class '10; appointed Assist. Prof. at his alma mater; incriminated in '20 for having published Kuropotkin's socialistic theories and imprisoned; sent to Europe by the Institute and stayed there from '21 to 23. Works; History of Socialist Views, Sketch of Kuropotkin, etc. Add. Ohara Institute, Osaka. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 861](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n861/mode/1up))

**Moriya, Gohei** Proprietor of Moriya & Co. and Dir. of the Nippon Pig-Iron Mfg. Co.; b. 1866 in Okayama-ken; grad. from Keio in '93, and entering Mitsui Firm the same year, left it to open in '02 the present firm which deals in machines, electric apparatuses, steel, etc. Add. Kyobashi Sanjukken-bori, Tokyo. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 861](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n861/mode/1up))

**Moroi, Rokuro** ex-Minister to Argentina ('24-26); b. 1872 in Saitama-ken; edu. at Imp. Tokyo Univ. in Law, class '96 and passed the Higher Civil Service in the same year and the Dip. Service in '99; Eleve Con. at Shanghai, London, etc.; Con. '01; Sec. at the head office '06; 1st Emb. Sec. to Berlin '12; recalled home on the outbreak of the War and was in charge of the Correspondence Section; Con.-Gen. at Honolulu in '16. Add. Tokyo. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 861](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n861/mode/1up))

**Moroi, Shiro** Mng.-Dir. of the Toa Flour Mill; b. 1869 in Saitama-ken; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '90; entered Keifu R'ly. Co., became Manager of the present Co. on its establishment '06. Add. Hongo Yushima-Shinhanacho, Tokyo. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 861](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n861/mode/1up))

**Motoda, Hajime** M.P. of unbroken record, lawyer; b. 1856 in Oita; was a leader of the National Association organized in '92, as organ of the clan statesmen; joined late Prince Ito when he organized the Seiyukai in '00; Vice-President of the House of Rep., '98-02; Director of Colonization Bureau, '11; Minister of Communications, '13-14; Min. of Railways, '18-21. Add. Kojimachi Kioicho, Tokyo. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 861](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n861/mode/1up))

**Motoda, Sakunoshin** B.A., M.A., Ph. D. (Philadelphia), Dir. of Rikkyo Univ.; b. 1862 in Kurume; studied Divinity, Philosophy etc. at Kenyon College, University of Penn. and Columbia University '86-96; attended the Jubilee Convention of Y.M.C.A. held at Boston '01; travelled through India as a lecturer in '08; attended the General Convention of the Church in America held in '10. Add. Nishi-Okubo, near Tokyo. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 861](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n861/mode/1up))

**Motoji, Niikuma** D.C.L., Dir. Prison Affair Bureau, Justice Dep't; b. 1876 in Kagoshima-ken; grad. from Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., in '02; was Public Procurator of the Court of Appeal, and Judge of the Court of Cassation before his appointment to the present post. Add. Shimo-Shibuya, Tokyo. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 861](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n861/mode/1up))

**Motoyama, Haku-un** sculptor; b. 1871 in Kochi; studied under Prof. Takamura Ko-un and at Tokyo Fine Art Academy '95; filled for a short while the chair of assist. teacher at the alma mater; has since been engaged in casting bronze images of modern notables in the grand work of the Restoration, and has already finished images of Count Goto, Prince Ito, and some others. Add. Koishikawa Haramachi, Tokyo. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 861](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n861/mode/1up))

**Motoyama, Hikoichi** Pres. of the Osaka Mainichi and Tokyo Nichi-nichi, Dir. of Nankai R'ly Co., Meiji Life Ins. Co., &c.; b. 1853 in Kumamoto; grad. from Keio-gijuku; once served in the Fujita Firm; established the Osaka Mainichi in '92. Add. Osaka Mainichi, Osaka. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 862](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n862/mode/1up))

**Murai, Teinosuke** Ph. D. (Yale), Pres. of Murai Bank, Dir. of Japan Fertilizer Co., etc.; b. 1870 in Wakayama-ken; adopted by the Murai family. Add. Shimoshibuya, Tokyo. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 862](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n862/mode/1up))

**Murai, Yasutaka** Dir. of the Murai Firm, Ltd., Tokyo, and its agent in New York for some thirty years; b. 1854 in Tokyo; grad. from Keio about '80 and entered the firm which exports raw silk, porcelains, and sundries; married an American lady. Add. Murai-gumi, New York. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 862](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n862/mode/1up))

**Murakami, Naojiro** D. Litt., Dir. Tokyo Academy of Music; b. 1868 in Oita-ken; edu. in history, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '95; Prof. of Spanish in Tokyo School of Foreign Languages, '00; Dir. of the same School, 08. Add. Nagasaki-mura, near Tokyo. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 862](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n862/mode/1up))

**Murakami, Ryukichi** ex-Dir. Fishery Bureau, Dept. Agr. & For.; b. 1877; grad. at Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '02; passed Higher Civil Serv. Exam.; Prof. of the Fishery Institute and Dir. Patent Bureau. Add. Kol. Myoga-dani, Tokyo. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 862](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n862/mode/1up))

**Murakami, Senjo** D. Litt., Emeritus Prof. of the Tokyo Imp. Univ. and Principal of the Toyo Girls' High School; b. 1851 in Fukui-ken. A well known scholar of Buddhism. Add. Koishikawa Hayashi-cho, Tokyo. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 862](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n862/mode/1up))

**Murayama, Ryuhei** former proprietor of the Tokyo and Osaka Asahi Shimbun; b. 1850 at Wakayama; has by his shrewd business management raised the two Asahi to the present stage of importance; once sat in Parliament for Osaka-fu. Add. Osaka Asahi Office, Osaka. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 862](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n862/mode/1up))

**Murobuse, Takanobu** social and democratic writer; b. in Izu 1889; edu. at Meiji Univ. which he left unfinished; started journalistic career connected with the Jiji, the Asahi, and other dailies and periodicals; sent to Europe by the Kaizo magazine (Reconstruction) in '20 and succeeded in bringing Einstein over to lecture. Works: Lecture on Democracy; About Capitalism; Guild Socialism, Decay of Civilization, etc. Add. Omori, near Tokyo. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 862](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n862/mode/1up))

**Murota, Yoshibumi** Lord-in-Waiting of the Kinkei Hall, Chairman of the Board of Directors of the Tokiwa Savings Bank, Pres. of the Naikoku Savings Bank, the Nippon Military Service Ins. Co., Ltd.; Dir. of the Dai-Nippon Artificial Fertilizer Co., the Kanegafuchi Spinning Co.; b. 1847 in Mito City; early entered the Foreign Office, and was Minister to Mexico and Peru; retiring from the government service, entered business; appoint. Lord-in-Waiting of the Kinkei-Hall. Add. Odawara-machi, Kanagawa-ken. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 862](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n862/mode/1up))

**Mushakoji, Kimitomo** Vice., Minister to Rumania since '25; b. in 1882, ninth descendant of an old courtier house; edu. at Imp. Tokyo Univ. in Law, class '07 and passed the Diplomatic Service Exam.; Secretary, Sectional Chief, and Emb. Counsellor at Brussels and then at Berlin in succession. Add. Bucharest. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 862](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n862/mode/1up))

**Mushakoji, Sane-atsu** novelist and founder of a "New Community" in the far-off Hyuga; b. 1885; edu. at the Peers Sch. and the Imp. Tokyo Univ. in Literature, but left unfinished. Works: Life in a New Village; His Sister, etc. Add. Kishiromura, Hyuga. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 863](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n863/mode/1up))

**Muto, Sanji** M.P., Pres. of Kanegafuchi Spinning Mill; b. 1867 in Gifu; grad. from Keio-gijuku '84; studied in America, '85-87; entered the Mitsui Firm '90; Mng. Kanegafuchi Mill then controlled by the Mitsui and has since been connected with it; rewarded with Blue Ribbon Medal in '18 for his service in promoting spinning industry; attended the Inter. Labor Conference at Washington '20; organized the Business-men's Party in '23. Add. Tarumi near Kobe. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 863](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n863/mode/1up))

**Mutsu, Hirokichi** Count, ex-Min. to Belgium; b. 1869, son of the late Count Mutsu of the treaty revision fame; studied at Cambridge and Inner Temple, '93; Translator, Foreign Office, '94; passed Dip. and Con. Service Exam. '95; 3rd Secretary of Legation (U.S.A.) '96; Sec. at Rome '00; 1st Emb. Sec. at London '04; Emb. Counsellor at Rome, '12; transferred to Belgium '15 but soon resigned. Add. Kamakura, Kanagawa-ken. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 863](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n863/mode/1up))

**Nabeshima, Chokuei** Marq., Mem. House of Peers; b. 1872, of an ancient Daimyo family and succeeded to the title in '23; his sister is Princess Nashimoto, while his wife is Marquis Kuroda's sister. Add. Naka Shibuya, near Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 863](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n863/mode/1up))

**Nabeshima, Keijiro** Mem. House of Peers since '16 and ex-Minister to Belgium; b. 1860 in Saga; attache in London in '87; Sec. at head office in '92; on suite of Prince Arisugawa in Queen Victoria's Diamond Jubilee in '97; 1st Legation Sec. at Washington in '00 and at London in '02; Sec. at head office in '03; Chief of Foreign Affairs, Korea, in '07-09. Add. Azabu Higashi Torizaka, Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 863](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n863/mode/1up))

**Nagai, Matsuzo** Minister to Sweden, Norway, Denmark and Finland, since '23; b. 1877 in Aichi-ken; grad. from Imp. Tokyo Univ. '02; passed Dip. and Cons. Exam.; Eleve Consul at Tientsin, New York and Washington '03-07; Consul-Gen. at San Francisco, '08-12; Chief of Telegraph Section (For. Office) '16; Emb. Counsellor in London '17-21; Dir. Commercial Bureau (For. Office) '21-23; present post in '23. Add. Stockholm. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 863](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n863/mode/1up))

**Nagai, Nagayoshi** M.D. (Japan), Ph. D. (Berlin), Emeritus Prof. of Imp. Tokyo Univ. and Mem. of the Imp. Academy; b. 1845 in Tokushima-ken. Studied Chemistry and Pharmacy at Berlin Univ., '70-77; after four years service at the Home Office proceeded once more to Berlin for study '81-83 during which he was appointed Assist.-Prof. at the alma mater. Has since devoted himself to his speciality as Prof. of the Imp. Univ., Mem. of the Central Sanitary Committee, Home Office, Pres. of the Japan Pharmacopeia Society, etc. Add. Aoyama Minami-cho, Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 863](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n863/mode/1up))

**Nagai, Ryutaro** M.P. (Minsei-to); b. in 1881 at Kanazawa; grad. from Waseda (politics) in '05, and further studied in England and Germany '06-09; Prof. at Waseda Univ.; was Parl. Counsellor of For. Office '26-27. Add. Totsuka Tobichi, near Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 863](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n863/mode/1up))

**Nagamatsu, Atsusuke** 2nd Baron, Member of House of Peers, Mng.-Dir. of Tokyo Fire, Marine, Transport Ins. Co.; b. 1864 in Tokyo, eldest son of Chokan, 1st Baron; studied at the Peers' School, Imp. Tokyo Univ., and Berlin, whence he returned in '86; was for a while Prof. at the Peers School. Add. Aoyama Minami-cho, Tokyo. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 864](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n864/mode/1up))

**Nagao, Hampei** Ex-Dir. of Electric Bureau of Tokyo City; b. in 1865 in Niigata-ken; grad. from Eng. Coll. Tokyo Imp. Univ. '91; was in the service of the Formosan Government-General for many years; appointed Engineer of Gov't Railways in '10; Dir. of Central Rly Bureau, '16. Add. Nakano, near Tokyo. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 864](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n864/mode/1up))

**Nagaoka, Gaishi** M.P., Lt.-Gen. (ret.); b. 1858 in Yamaguchi; Sub-Lieut. '73; entered Mil. Staff Coll. '83; Maj.-Gen. '02; Vice-Chief of Staff during Russo-Japanese War; Bureau Chief in War Office; Lieut.-Gen. '09 and Com'der of 16th Div.; retired from active service '15; toured Europe and America '20; was Vice-Pres. of the Imp. Aero Ass'n. till '27. Add. Harajuku, Sendagaya, Tokyo. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 864](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n864/mode/1up))

**Nagaoka, Hantaro** D. Sc., Hon. D. Sc. (Cambridge), Prof. of Physics at the Imp. Tokyo Univ., Mem. Imp. Academy; b. 1865 in Nagasaki-ken; grad. from Theoretical Physics at above institution, class '87; Assis. Prof. at the alma mater, '90-93; further studied at the Univ. Hall and in Germany. Interpreted Einstein's lectures addressed in Tokyo in '12; Succeeded in converting mercury into gold after 15 years' researches and announced this discovery early in '24; and also obtained a species of platinum from mercury in the same year. Lectured in Belgium, '25. Add. Coll. Sc., Imp. Tokyo Univ. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 864](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n864/mode/1up))

**Nagaoka, Haruichi** D.L., Ambassador in Berlin since '26; b. in Tokyo 1877; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '00 and also from a French Coll. in '04; passed Higher Civil Service Exam. and entered Foreign Office; Emb. Secretary at Paris, etc.; Min. to Czecho-Slovakia '21; Minister to Holland '24; Dir. of Treaty Bureau, For. Office, '25-26. Add. Japanese Embassy, Berlin. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 864](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n864/mode/1up))

**Nagaoka, Ryuichiro** Dir. Social Affairs Bureau, Home Office; b. in Tokyo; grad. from Law Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ., in '08; was Secretary of the Home Dept. and then Dir. of Civil Eng. Bureau, before appointment to the present post; visited Europe and America on official mission '27. Add. Shiba Shirokane, Tokyo. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 864](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n864/mode/1up))

**Nagashima, Washitaro** Barrister in Tokyo; b. in Okayama-ken in 1868; grad. Law Imp. Tokyo Univ. '90; Junior Councillor of Justice but resigned '93; and began practice; travelled abroad and was Japanese delegate at the International Parliamentary Conference in '09. Add. Tsukiji, Tokyo. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 864](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n864/mode/1up))

**Nagata, Hidejiro** Crown Mem. House of Peers since '23; b. 1876 in Hyogo-ken; studied at the 1st Higher Sch., passed the Exam. for the Bar; Principal of a middle school and local Educational Inspector; entered Home Office; Prof. at a Gov. School for a time; Dir. Police Bureau '13-18; Deputy Mayor of Tokyo '21-23; Mayor '23-24. Add. Zoshigaya, Tokyo. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 864](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n864/mode/1up))

**Nagatomi, Yukichi** ex-Pres. Nippon Yusen Kaisha: b. 1868, Awa, Chiba-ken; grad. from Tokyo Higher Commercial School; further studied at Antwerp Com. School and returned home '95; lecturer at his alma mater; entered next year the N.Y.K. and its Director in '19. Add. Araijuku Sanno, near Tokyo. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 865](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n865/mode/1up))

**Nagaya, Junji** Dir. of Tokyo School of Foreign Languages; b. 1876 in Gifu-ken; grad. from Lit. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ., '97; was Prof. of the 4th High School and Hiroshima Higher Normal School; then School Inspector; sent abroad for observation of educational affairs '27. Add. Nakano, Tokyo. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 865](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n865/mode/1up))

**Nagayo, Mataro** M.D., Prof. of Pathology & Patho. Anatomy, Imp. Tokyo Univ., and Dir. of the Univ. Infectious Disease Laboratory; b. 1878 in Tokyo, 3rd son of the late Baron Nagayo, father of sanitary work in Japan. Edu.: grad. from Coll. Med. above institution, class '04, and further studied at the Univ. Hall and in Germany till '09. Has been connected with the alma mater ever since with a short break in '06-07 when he was attached to the Bodyguard Division; attended the Tropical Disease Conference at Batavia in '21. Add. Coll. Med., Imp. Univ. Tokyo. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 865](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n865/mode/1up))

**Nagayo, Teizo** Dir. Yokosuka Iron Works, Taihei Cotton Spinning, etc.; b. 1874 in Tokyo; studied at Peers' School; a clerk of Silk Exporting Co., Yokohama; studied economics at Pennsylvania University '03; served at the N.Y.K. Seattle Branch; the Chief of Mogi Trading Dept. and Mng.-Dir. of Mogi Co. till its liquidation. Add. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 865](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n865/mode/1up))

**Naito, Hikokichi** Manager of Matsuya Dry Goods Store since '94; Mem. of the Tokyo Chamber of Commerce; b. 1865 in Yamanashi-ken; studied at the now defunct Dojinsha; studied English in U.S.A. in '85; returned home and entered the Firm in '93. Add. Matsuya Dry Goods Store, Ginza, Tokyo. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 865](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n865/mode/1up))

**Naito, Kyukan** Crown Mem. House of Peers, and ex-Pres. of Nippon Petroleum Co., Dir. of Central Pet. Co., etc.; b. 1859 in Niigata-ken; elected Mem. of Prefectural Assembly at his 20th year; M.P. in '94; organized Nippon Pet. Co. in '88; despatched on official mission to inspect petroleum business to America and Europe in '97 and again to U.S.A. in '04. Add. Ushigome shimo-Miyabicho, Tokyo. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 865](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n865/mode/1up))

**Naito, Tachu** D. Eng., Prof. at Waseda Univ. since '10. Mem. of Gov. Earthquake Committee; b. 18[?]6 in Yamanashi-ken; edu. in Architecture, Imp. Tokyo Univ., class '10; further studied Ferro-concrete and allied construction at the Institute of Technology, Boston, in '16-18. Works: Earthquake-proof Construction of Skeleton Structure, Aug. '24; The Theory of Structure and of Building Construction, 9th June, '25 (Both in Japanese). Add. Waseda Univ. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 865](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n865/mode/1up))

**Naito, Torajiro** D. Litt., Prof. of Kyoto Imp. Univ.; b. in Akita-ken; grad. from Lit. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ.; was on the staff of the Osaka Asahi Shimbun and its Peking correspondent for several years; is a well known authority on Chinese history. Add Kamikyo-ku, Kyoto. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 865](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n865/mode/1up))

**Nakagawa, Kojuro** Ex-Pres. of the Bank of Taiwan; b. 186[?] in Kyoto-fu; grad. from Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '93; was Councillor of the Education Dep't and Secretary to Premier (Prince Saionji). Add. Totsuka-machi, near Tokyo. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 865](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n865/mode/1up))

**Nakagawa, Sho[?]a** former Vice-Pres. of Tokyo Subway Co., (resigned '27); b. 1881; grad. at Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '05; Personal Secretary to the Minister of Com. the same year; Secr. of the Govt. Rly. Board till '20; Dir. Traffic Bureau, Rly Dept. '20-24; Vice-Minister in '24. Add. Ushigome Kita-machi, Tokyo. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 866](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n866/mode/1up))

**Nakagawa, Suekichi, M.A. (Yale)** Mng.-Dir. of Furukawa Bank; b. in Shiga-ken 1874; studied at Waseda but unfinished and further studied political economy at Yale; entered Furukawa Firm '08 as Chief Accountant of the Ashio Copper Mine; transferred to the Dept. Chief of the Firm. He is an expert in jujutsu. Add. Takanawa-Minamicho, Tokyo. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 866](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n866/mode/1up))

**Nakahashi, Tokugoro** Minister of Com. & Industry, President of Ujigawa Electric, Japan Lime Nitrogen Cos.; Auditor of South Manchuria Rly. and Japan-China S.S. Cos.; b. 18[?]0 at Kanazawa; grad. from Imperial Tokyo Univ. '84; was an official in the Com. Dep't where he occupied the post of Director of the Railway Bureau; resigned about '98 to take charge of Osaka Shosen Kaisha till 1916; elected M.P. for Kanazawa city in '17; Minister of Education '18-22; Add. Koj. Nakarokubancho, Tokyo. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 866](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n866/mode/1up))

**Nakai, Reisaku** Chief of Government Iron Foundry since '25; b. 1879 in Kumamoto; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '03; entered the Dept. Agr. and Com. where he was Secy. of one Bureau after another; Dir. of Patent Bureau and of Forestry Bureau; and then Vice-Min. Add. Yawata, Fukuoka-ken. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 866](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n866/mode/1up))

**Nakajima, Kumakichi, Baron (2nd)** Mem. of the House of Peers since '04; Mng.-Dir. of Toyo Iron Foundry, etc.; b. 1872 in Yokohama; son of late Baron Nobuyuki Nakajima, the first Chairman of the House of Representatives and Minister to Rome; grad. from the Tokyo Higher Commercial School and entered the stock exchange '97; private secretary to Prince Katsura and Marquis Saionji, as Premiers '02-05; travelled abroad '05; is interested in all the undertakings of the Furukawa family. Add. Aoyama-Kitamachi, Tokyo. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 866](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n866/mode/1up))

**Nakamatsu, Morio** Patent Attorney and Barrister; b. 1865 in Wakayama; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '91; entered the Dep't. of Agr. and Com. as Councillor and Patent Examiner, then, as Sectional Chief of the Bureau; Dir. of Patent Bureau '09-13. Add. Omori near Tokyo. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 866](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n866/mode/1up))

**Nakamigawa, Jirokichi** Dir. Nippon Silk (Kempu) Co., etc; b. 1883; edu. at Keio, and in England. Add. Azabu Kita-higakubo, Tokyo. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 866](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n866/mode/1up))

**Nakame, Satoru** Dir. Osaka Foreign Lang. Sch. since '21; b. 1874 in Miyagi-ken; grad. from Imp. Tokyo Univ. (German Literature), '03; studied in Europe; Prof. of 4th High Sch., Hiroshima Higher Normal Sch., Dir. of Matsuyama High Sch. and then the present post; toured abroad on official mission in '27. Add. Osaka. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 866](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n866/mode/1up))

**Nakamura, Kichizo** Novelist and dramatist; b. 1877 in Shiga-ken; grad. from Lit., Waseda Univ., '03; further studied in Princeton and Columbia Univs. '0[?]-09. Add. Sugamo Miyanaka, near Tokyo. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 866](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n866/mode/1up))

**Nakamura, Naojiro** Prop. of Hatbara Paper Store dealing in Japanese paper fancy goods, Tokyo; b. 18[?]0 and studied German in Tokyo Foreign Language School and further in Europe. Add. Nihombashi Tori 1-chome, Tokyo. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 866](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n866/mode/1up))

**Nakamura, Seinan** Dr. Sc., ex-Dir. of Central Meteorological Observatory; b. 1855 in Yamaguchi-ken; edu. at Imp. Tokyo Univ. and sent to Europe to study meteorology '86-89; Expert to the Observatory which sent him abroad '99; retired in '23. Add. Ushigome Minami-cho, Tokyo. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n867/mode/1up))

**Nakamura, Shingo** D.L. (Japan) Prof. at Tokyo Univ. of Commerce, Waseda Univ., etc.; b. 1870 in Niigata Prefecture; grad. from Law, Tokyo Imp. in '90 and further at the University Hall; then in England, Germany and France (International Law and History of Diplomacy). Add. Yoyogi Sanya, near Tokyo. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n867/mode/1up))

**Nakamura, Takashi** M.P. from Wakayama-ken; grad. from Tokyo High Com. School and passed the Diplomatic and Consular Service Examination, '06; Consul-General at New York, '17; Director of the Com. Affairs Bureau, Foreign Office; then Ambassador to Argentina and later Vice-Min. of Foreign Affairs. Add. Ushigome Suido-cho, Tokyo. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n867/mode/1up))

**Nakamura, Tatsutaro** D. E. Emeritus Professor of Imperial Tokyo University; b. in Tokyo in 1860; edu. at now defunct Eng. College in '82; assist. architect in building the Imperial Palace '83-86; Professor of the present University combining a post in the Home Office; studied in Europe in '92-98; sent in '97 to Assam, India, to study architecture as affected by earthquake, and also to Naples in '09 on a similar mission. Add. Koishikawa Haramachi, Tokyo. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n867/mode/1up))

**Nakamura, Yujiro** Baron, Lieut.-General (ret), Privy Councillor since '22; ex-Min. Imp. Household and ex-Gov.-General of Kwantung; b. 1852 in Miye-ken; Sub-Lieut. in '74 and through gradual promotion, once held the post of Vice-Minister of War; Head of the Military Affairs Board and Chief of the Govt. Iron Foundry in '06-14; Governor of South Manchuria Railway, '15-17. Add. Yotsuya Nakacho, Tokyo. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n867/mode/1up))

**Nakanishi, Inosuke** socialist and labor leader and writer; b. 1893 in Kyoto-fu but with no regular education; worked his way as mechanic and journalist; has been sentenced three times for disturbing order; an active member of the Transport Labor Union. Works: Growth in the Red Soil, Look Behind Your Back, etc. Add. Yoyogi Sanya, near Tokyo. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n867/mode/1up))

**Nakano, Seigo** M.P. (Kenseikai) for Fukuoka; contributor to journals; b. 1885 in Fukuoka-ken; edu. at Waseda in Politics, class '09; connected with the Tokyo Asahi, resigning which he started the Oriental Review. Works: The World Politics. At the Seat of the Peace Conference, etc. Add. Sendagaya Harajuku, Tokyo. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n867/mode/1up))

**Nakano, Teppei** Mng.-Dir. of Japan Petroleum Co., Ltd.; b. 1869 in Niigata-ken; grad. from Law, Waseda Univ., '91, and has since been connected with the Co. Add. Nishi Okubo, Tokyo. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n867/mode/1up))

**Nakano, Torakichi** M.P. (Kenseikai); b. 1879 in Fukushima-ken; grad. '01 from Law, Waseda Univ.; Police-inspector in Hokkaido, Formosa, and Korea. Add. Fukushima. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n867/mode/1up))

**Nakayama, Hidesaburo** D. Eng., Prof. Civil Engineering at the Imp. Univ. of Tokyo since '90; b. in 1867 in Aichi-ken; grad. from the University with honours in '88; engineer to the Kwansai R'ly Co. for some years; studied further his speciality in Germany and other countries in '96-98. Add. Hongo Nishikatamachi, Tokyo. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n867/mode/1up))

**Nakazato, Juji** Vice-Adm. (retired), President of the Karafuto Petroleum Co. Ltd. created in 192[?] as semi-official organ for exploiting the oil field conceded by the Soviet Government in '24; b. in 1871 in Yamagata-ken; grad. from the Naval Cadet School, '93; steadily promoted till he was appointed a senior staff officer of the General Staff as a Rear-Adm. in 1918; Vice-Adm. '24 and appointed Commander of the Maizuru Naval Port. Add. Aoyama Minami-machi, Tokyo. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 868](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n868/mode/1up))

**Nambu, Koshin** Mem. House of Peers, Counsellor of Imp. Household Dept.; b. 1864 in Kyoto, adopted by Baron Kameo Nambu; grad. at Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '90; Secretary of the Home Office '91. Once Dir. Forestry Bureau of the Household; Director of Public Works Bureau in '07. Add. Azabu Hommuru-cho, Tokyo. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 868](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n868/mode/1up))

**Nambu, Kyugo** D. Eng. (Japan), Director of the Mitsubishi Firm; b. in 1855 in Fukui; grad. at Imp. Tokyo University in '74; was among the first batch of students sent abroad by the Gov. for prosecution of study, he studying mining in U.S.A. '75-[?]0; was engaged the following year by the Mitsubishi to take charge of Takashima coal mine. Add. Ushigome Benten-cho, Tokyo. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 868](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n868/mode/1up))

**Nannichi, Tsunetaro** Principal of the Toyama High School; b. 1871; was Prof. of the Peers' School and Lecturer at the Third High School; the present post in '24. Add. Toyama City. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 868](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n868/mode/1up))

**Naoki, Rintaro** D. Eng., Dir. of the Reconstruction Bureau '24-25, adviser to the same since Sept. '25; b. 1876 in Kobe; grad. from Civil Eng., Imp. Tokyo Univ., class '09; Engineer to the Tokyo City Office the same year and was despatched abroad to inspect harbor construction work in Europe and America, returning home '03, and appointed chief of public work section of the city; Eng. of the Finance Dept.; attached to the Construction Bureau, '06; in charge of the Yokohama harbor work, '08; Eng. to the Tokyo City Office, '11; chief of the Osaka Harbor Work Office and also of the City Planning Office, till '24 when he was appointed Dir. of the Reconstruction Bureau. Add. Reconstruction Bureau. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 868](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n868/mode/1up))

**Nara, Takeji** Gen. and Chief Aide-de-Camp to the Emperor; b. 1868 in Tochigi-ken; Sub-Lieut. Artillery '89; the present rank in '17; took part in the Sino-Japanese and Russo-Japanese Wars; Chief of Staff and then Commander of the Japanese Garison in China '13-17; called back home '17 and represented the Japanese Army in the Paris Peace Conference '18; accompanied the Crown Prince to Europe '21. Add. Imp. Household Dept. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 868](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n868/mode/1up))

**Naruse, Masayasu** ex-Pres. 15th Bank, Dir. Chiyoda Fire Ins., etc.; 1868; studied at Keio and then in America; early took to banking. Add. Shirokane Sanko-cho, Shiba. His brother, Masayuki, b. 1876, also studied at Keio and is now in business. Add. Horo-o-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 868](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n868/mode/1up))

**Nedzu, Kaichiro** Crown Mem. House of Peers nominated in '27 Pres. Union Beer Brewing Co., Dir. of Tobu R'ly Co., etc.; b. 1860 in Yamanashi-ken; took active part in village and provincial affairs; was one of the businessmen who visited U.S.A. in the fall of '09 at the special invitation of American Chambers of Commerce; M.P. for some time. Add. Aoyama Minami-cho, Tokyo. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 868](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n868/mode/1up))

**Nemoto, Sho** ex-M.P., Dir. of the Teikoku Oil Co., etc., auditor of the Dye-Stuff Manufacturing Co.; b. 1851 in Ibaraki-ken; studied in America; sent by the Government to Mexico and Brazil to inquire into emigration affairs '03; has actively espoused the cause of free education and anti-juvenile smoking and drinking. Add. Mita Shikokumachi, Tokyo. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n869/mode/1up))

**Niho, Kamematsu** D.C.L., Prof. of Law Coll., Imp. Kyoto Univ.; b. in Ise 1868; grad. from Law Coll., Tokyo '93; studied in Germany '97-00. Add. Imp. Kyoto University. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n869/mode/1up))

**Niida, Masutaro** D.C.L., Dean of Law Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ. '19-21; b. 1868 in Fukushima-ken; grad. at Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '93; sent to Germany for study '97-00; Prof. of the Imp. Kyoto Univ. and then of his alma mater in '08; opened law office in '21. Add. Kci. Kobinata-Daimachi, Tokyo. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n869/mode/1up))

**Niijima, Yoshinao** D. Dend., Prof. of Forestry at the Imp. Hokkaido Univ.; b. 1871; edu. at the Sapporo Agr. Coll. which has been expanded to the present full University, class '94, Forestry course; granted the doctorate in '90; has charge of the Experimental Forests attached to the University. Add. Sapporo, Hokkaido. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n869/mode/1up))

**Ninagawa, Arata** D.L., Mem. of the Board of Governors of the League of Red Cross Societies; Dir. of the League of Nations Association of Japan. Grad. from the Law College of the Tokyo Imp. Univ.; was once Legal Adviser to the Korean Govt.; attached to the Japan Red Cross Society, '18. Add. Nakashibuya, Tokyo. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n869/mode/1up))

**Nishida, Hirotaro** D. Eng., Dir. and Prof. of the Kiryu Higher Tech. School, Prop. of the Nishida Laboratory; b. 1877 in Tokyo; grad. in applied chemistry, Tokyo Imp. Univ.; further studied at the School of Technology in Manchester, and Ecole de Chimie, Mulhouse, Alsace; was Prof. of the Nagoya High Technical Coll.; Mng.-Dir. of the Nippon Celluloid Co. Add. Kojimachi Junichome, Tokyo. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n869/mode/1up))

**Nishida, Ikutaro** D.Litt., Prof. at Imp. Kyoto Univ.; b. 1870 in Ishikawa-ken; edu. at Imp. Tokyo Univ. (Electic Course) in Philosophy, class '94; taught at the Yamaguchi and the Fourth High School before his appointment to the present post. Works: What Is Righteousness?, Problems of Consciousness, Idealistic Philosophy of the Day, Art and Morals, etc. Add. Kyoto Univ. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n869/mode/1up))

**Nishikubo, Hiromichi** ex-Mayor of Tokyo, Mem. House of Peers nom. '16; b. in 1863 at Saga; grad. at Law, Govt. Law School; Prof. at Military Staff College; sent to England in '02; Prefectural Governor till '14; Superintendent of Metropolitan Police Board '15-16; Mayor of Tokyo '27. Add. Tokyo. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n869/mode/1up))

**Nishino, Gen** Mem. House of Peers, Pres. Fifteenth Bank; b. in Ibaraki; grad. at Law Imp. Tokyo Univ. '02; Dir. of the Account Bureau before promotion to the present post. Sent to London in '05 and again to Europe in '07; Vice-Min. Finance Dept. '23-24. Add. Ushigome Sadowaracho, Tokyo. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n869/mode/1up))

**Nishino, Keinosuke** Pres. of the Shirokiya Dep't Store, Ltd., Dir. of several other cos.; b. 1864 in Kyoto; grad. from Keio Univ.; once Manager of the Sanyo Railway Co.; then became Mng.-Dir. of the Imperial Theatre, resigning the post in '13. Add. Shirokane Sanko-cho, Shiba-ku, Tokyo. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n869/mode/1up))

**Nishiwaki, Saisaburo** Pres. of Nishiwaki Bank, Dir. of several other banks & cos.; b. 1880 in Niigata of a millionaire family; studied at the Peers' School and Cambridge (class '08). Add. Ushigome Wakamatsu-cho, Tokyo. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 870](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n870/mode/1up))

**Nitobe, Inazo** Crown Mem. House of Peers since '27; D. Agr., D.L., late of League of Nations Office, Geneva, Prof. at Tokyo Imp. Univ.; b. 1861 in Iwate-ken and grad. Sapporo Agr. Coll. '81; studied political economy at John Hopkins' Univ. and at Bonn, Halle, Berlin; Prof. at the Sapporo College for several years after his return; official of Formosan Govt., which he left in '04 to fill chair of Economy at the Univ. of Kyoto; selected as first Japan-America exchange professor in '11. Works: "Bushido," published at Philadelphia in '98; "Nogyo Honron" (Principle of Agriculture) and others. Add. Kobinatadaimachi, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 870](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n870/mode/1up))

**Noboru, Naotaka** (Penname Shomu), Russian scholar and writer; b. in 1888 in O-shima, Kagoshima; edu. at the Orthodox Church Divinity School, class '03; connected with the Osaka Asahi '05; Instructor of Russian at Waseda and military schools from '12 to 16; in the service of the Home Office '19; visited Europe in '13. Works: Russian Thoughts and Literature of Today, Russia and Russians, Russian Revolution and Socialist Movement, Russian Proletariat Literature, Master Writers of Russia, etc. Add. Ushigome Wakamatsu-cho, Tokyo. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 870](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n870/mode/1up))

**Noda, Yoshio** D. Litt., Dir. Osaka High Sch. since '22; b. in Fukuoka-ken in 1873; grad. at Philosophy, Imp. Tokyo Univ.; studied in England, Germany and U.S.A; Prof. Hiroshima Higher Normal Sch.; Educational Inspector; Dir. of Nara Girls Higher Normal Sch., etc. Add. Osaka High School. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 870](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n870/mode/1up))

**Nogami, Toshio** Litt. D. and Prof. at Imp. Kyoto Univ.; b. 1882 in Sado; edu. at First High School and Imp. Tokyo Univ. in Psychology, class '06, and further in Europe and at Leipzig under Wundt from '13 to '16 and was the last Japanese under him at that time; crossed to America where he attended Dr. Stanley Hall's class at Clark Univ. Works: Impression of Education in Europe and America, Lectures on Juvenile Psychology, etc. Add. Kyoto Univ. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 870](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n870/mode/1up))

**Noguchi, Hideyo** M.D. and D. Sch., Prof. at the Rockefeller Medical Institute, N. Y.; b. 1876 in Fukushima-ken, a son of a poor farmer; secured a medical licence after hard study at a private med. school; then an assistant at Dr. Kitazato's Laboratory; studied many years in U.S.A., etc.; recipient of prize of Acad. of Japan in '15. He temporarily returned home '15, and was candidate recommended by the Nobel Prize Council in '14. Publications; "Snake Venoms," '09, "Serum Diagnois of Syphilis," "Butyric Acid Test," '10. Add. Manhattan Av., New York, U.S.A. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 870](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n870/mode/1up))

**Noguchi, Yonejiro** known as Yone Noguchi in literary circles in both America and England, Lecturer at Keio Univ.; b. 1875 at Tsushima, Aichi-ken; studied at Keio Univ., and in U.S.A., where he was patronized by Joaquin Miller but mostly self-taught as far as his literary ability is concerned. Delivered lectures in England in '14. Publications: "From the Eastern Sea," "Lafcadio Hearn in Japan," "Hiroshige," "Korin," etc. Add. Nakano, near Tokyo. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 870](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n870/mode/1up))

**Nomura, Kichisaburo** Rear-Adm., Vice-Chief of the Naval Board of Command since '26; b. in 1877 in Wakayama-ken; graduated from the Naval Cadet School in '98; on the Commission for bringing home the Mikasa from England, '01; took part in the Russo-Japanese war; Attache in Austria-Hungary and Germany, '08-11; Attache at Washington, '15-18; Adjutant to the Chief Naval Delegate to the Peace Conference at Paris and the Disarmament Conference at Washington; Rear-Adm. and commander of Squadron to China, '22-25; relieved of the post and attached to the Naval Board of Command; then Dir. of Education Bureau (Navy Dep't.) '25-26. Add. Navy Dept., Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 871](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n871/mode/1up))

**Nomura, Ryutaro** D. Eng. (Japan), Pres. Tokyo Subway Co.; b. in 1859 at Ogaki; grad. from Civil Eng. Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '81; Eng. of Tokyo Prefectural Office '81-86; studied in Europe and America as R'ly. Engineer '96-98; promoted Chief-Eng. '10; Vice-Pres. Imp. Govt. R'lys. '12; President of the South Manchuria R'ly, 1919-20. Add. Akasaka Shinsakamachi, Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 871](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n871/mode/1up))

**Nonaka, Kiyoshi** ex-Gov. of Bank of Chosen; b. 1872; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '98; passed Higher Civil Service Exam.; Dir. of Kobe Customs Office; Dir. of Monopoly Bureau '18-24; Gov. of Bank of Chosen '24-25. Add. Nakashibuya near Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 871](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n871/mode/1up))

**Nonomura, Kingoro** former Dir. of the Kawasaki Bank, Ltd.; Dir of the Kojimachi Bank, Ltd.; Aud. of the Tokyo Bankers' Club, etc.; b. 1869 in Oita-ken; grad. from Morioka Middle School and the Tokyo English School; began his career as a journalist; later became Educational Adviser to the Korean Govt.; then Private Secretary of Denzaburo Fujita; Manager of the Kitahama Bank; sectional chief of the Industrial Bank of Japan; Dir. of the South Manchuria Railway Co., Ltd., holding the last named post till '14. Add. Kami-ochiai, Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 871](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n871/mode/1up))

**Norisugi, Kaju** Dir. of the Matsuye Higher School; b. in Toyama-ken; grad. from Lit. Tokyo Imp. Univ., '04; School-Inspector and then Secretary of the Education Dep't; the present post in '24. Add. Matsuye, Shimane-ken. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 871](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n871/mode/1up))

**Nukada, Yutaka** M.D., Dir. of the Nukada Hospital and Teikoku Female Medical College (private); b. 1880 in Okayama-ken. Edu. at Coll. Med. Imp. Tokyo Univ., class '60; served a term of assist. in the Internal Med. Dep't., Univ. Hospital; further studied in Munchen, '07-09; re-entered his alma mater to conduct special researches, and the thesis he submitted soon after got him the degree in '13. Add Akasaka Hikawa-cho, Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 871](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n871/mode/1up))

**Oba, Jiro** Gen. (Retired); b. 1864 in Yamaguchi-ken; app. Sub-Lieut. in '86; gradually promoted and rose to full Gen. in '21; studied in the meantime at the Staff Coll.; was Div. Commander and Commander of Chosen Army till his appointment to Superintendent of Military Education Board in '23; was once a member of Supreme War Council. Add. Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 871](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n871/mode/1up))

**Obata, Torikichi** Amb. to Turkey since '25; b. 1873 in Ishikawa-ken; grad. at Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '97; passed dip. exam. '98 and served at Tientsin, Singapore, Vienna, London, etc.; Secy. at Peking '05; Consul at Chefoo and Tientsin; Counsellor at Peking Legation and then Dir. of Political Bureau. Foreign Office, '16-18; Minister to China '18-22. Add. Japanese Embassy, Turkey. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 871](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n871/mode/1up))

**Obayashi, Kenshiro** Mng. Dir. of the Obayashi-gumi, Ltd., Contractors for Civil Engineering and Architecture; b. 1885 in Fukushima-ken; grad. in architecture at Tokyo Imp. Univ. class '1911. Add. Mikage-machi, Hyogo-ken. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 872](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n872/mode/1up))

**Odagiri, Masunosuke** Director of Yokohama Specie Bank and Superintendent of its Branches in China; b. 1865 in Tokyo; sent by the Foreign Office to Tientsin in '86 to study Chinese; after having been in the Bank in '06 consular service in China, ultimately rising to General-Consulship at Shanghai. Add. Shiba Shirokane Sanko-cho, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 872](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n872/mode/1up))

**Ogasawara, Chokan** Count, Mem. House of Peers; b. 1885 of a former Daimyo family; studied at the Peers' Sch. and then at Cambridge; was once in the service of the Imp. Household; was Senior Counsellor to Navy Office and then Dir. Census Board till it was abolished in '23. Add. Ushigome Ichigaya Kawada-cho, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 872](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n872/mode/1up))

**Ogasawara, Naganeri** Viscount (cr. '84 by heritage), Rear-Adm. (ret.), Court Councillor; was b. 1867 of the family that formerly held the fief of Karatsu clan; grad. from the Naval Acad. in '88; took part in both Sino-Japanese and Russo-Japanese Wars. Add. Yo[illegible]hata, near Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 872](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n872/mode/1up))

**Ogata, Masakiyo** M.D., Dir. of Ogata Hospital of Women's Disease; b. 1864 in Ehime-ken; studied medicine at Imp. Tokyo Univ., then at Berlin Univ. and elsewhere '88-92. Chief of Obstetrics Dept. of Ogata Hospital (his adopted father's); started his own '02; Chairman of Osaka Medical Practitioner's Association '10, of Osaka Midwives Ass., etc. Add. Imabashi, Higashiku, Osaka. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 872](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n872/mode/1up))

**Ogawa, Gotaro** D.L., M.P., Prof. at Imp. Kyoto Univ. till '24; b. 1876 in Okayama-ken; edu. at Imp. Tokyo Univ., Law, class '03; Lecturer at Imp. Kyoto Univ. till '07; further studied Finance in Germany, France, etc. on Govt. order, '08-12; resumed duty at the Kyoto Univ.; attended the Carnegie Peace Foundation Conference held in '14 at Geneva. Works: Manuals of Political Economy; Lectures on Taxes; Lectures on Economics, etc., etc. Add. Imp. Kyoto Univ. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 872](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n872/mode/1up))

**Ogawa, Heikichi** Barrister, M.P. (Seiyukai), Minister of Railways since Apr. '27; b. in Nagano-ken in 1869; grad. at Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '92; Dir. Census Bureau on its creation in '20; left it in '22 when its status was lowered; Minister of Justice in the Coalition Cabinet '25. Add. Kojimachi Uchisaiwaicho, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 872](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n872/mode/1up))

**Ogawa, Masataka** ex-President of Imp. Tohoku Univ.; b. in Ehime-ken, 1865; grad. from the Coll. of Science, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '89; Prof. of the 1st High School and Tokyo Higher Normal School; Prof. of the Imp. Tohoku Univ. Add. Sendai. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 872](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n872/mode/1up))

**Ogino, Mototaro** Dir. Furukawa Elec. Industry Co.; b. 1874 in Okayama-ken and was adopted into the family; studied at Waseda and entered Furukawa Firm; branch manager at Osaka and Shanghai, and then Dir. of Business Section of the Head Office, etc. Stayed in England. Add. Koj. Ichi-Bancho, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 872](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n872/mode/1up))

**Oguri, Kozaburo** Vice-Adm. (ret.), former Com. of Maizuru Naval Station; b. in 1868 in Ishikawa-ken; Midshipman in '85; Section Chief of the Naval Construction Board, Naval Attache to Embassy at London. Dir. of Military Affairs Bur., Naval Department. Dir. of the Kure Naval Arsenal etc. Commanded the 3rd Squadron escorting the Crown Prince to and from Europe in '21. Add. Naval Dept., Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 872](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n872/mode/1up))

**Ohara, Magosaburo** Pres. of the Kurashiki Spinning Co., founder of the Ohara Institute for Research in Social Problems, also connected with many other business concerns; b. 1880 in Okayama-ken. Add. Kurashiki, Okayama-ken. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n873/mode/1up))

**Ohara, Senkichi** 2nd Baron, Dir. of the Construction Bureau, Imp. Household; b. in 1871; grad. at Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '96; Sec. of House of Peers; was Dir. of the Bureau of Supplies, Imp. Household before removal to the present post. Add. Koj. Naka-Rokuban-cho, Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n873/mode/1up))

**Ohara, Tatsumei** Pres. Yachiyo Life Ins., Teikoku Fire Ins., Niigata Paper-Mill, etc.; b. in Kumamoto-ken, '74; grad. at Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. 1900; was in the service of the Hypothec Bank. Toured abroad. Add. Zoshigaya Asahide, near Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n873/mode/1up))

**Ohashi, Shintaro** Crown Mem. House of Peers; Prop. of the Hakubunkan, a well-known publishing house, Pres. of Seoul Elec. Co., etc.; b. 1863, eldest son of the late Sahei, founder of the house; studied at the Dojinsha and was his father's right-hand man in carrying the publishing business to the prosperity it attained; founded the Ohashi Library; sat for a while in the House of Representatives for Tokyo; travelled abroad in '09; was on the businessmen's party that visited England in '21. Add Koj. Kami-Rokubancho, Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n873/mode/1up))

**Ohashi, Suisaki** gold medallist; b. 1865 in Gifu-ken; first studied painting under the late Watanabe Shokwa and then followed the Chinese style of southern school; won high-grade medals at domestic and foreign exhibitions, at Paris '00, St. Louis '03, and Anglo-Japanese Exhibition in London '10. Add. Ogaki, Mino. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n873/mode/1up))

**Ohno, Morie** Minister to Austria and Hungary; b. in 1878 at Tokuyama; edu. at Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., class '04; passed the Dip. and Con. Exam., '07; Eleve Consul in China, '07-10; Attache in Germany, '10, and 3rd Emb. Sec. '11; in Austria '12-14; 2nd Emb. Sec. in Italy, '16-17; Sec. Chief at the Head Office, '17-19; Con.-Gen. at Hamburg, '19-23; 1st Emb. Sec. and then Coun. in Germany, '23; Counsellor of the Legation in Vienna '26 and the present post '27. Add. Vienna. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n873/mode/1up))

**O-i, Narimoto** Gen. (ret.) b. 1863 at Yamaguchi; Sub-Lieut. in '81; entered the Mil. Staff Coll., in '86; studied in Germany, Major '97, Colonel '05 and Major-Gen. '09; took part both in the Japan-China and Japan-Russia Wars; Lieut.-Gen. '15; full General and Com. Siberian Expedition '19-21; Mem. Supreme Council of War till '23 when he was placed on retired list. Add. Horinouchi, Toyotama-gun, Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n873/mode/1up))

**Oishi, Hirokichi** Mng.-Dir. of the Mitsubishi Trading Co.; b. 1873 in Tokyo; grad. from Imp. Tokyo Univ. and entered Mitsubishi '98; went abroad with Baron Iwasaki, ex-Pres. of the Co. '03-06; present post May '18 when Mitsubishi was reorganized into a joint stock co. Add. Nakano, near Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n873/mode/1up))

**Oishi, Masami** a retired politician; b. 1855 in Kochi-ken; became follower of Count Itagaki, but left him '82; was arrested '85 about dynamite affairs, and confined some months; travelled abroad about '86; Min. Res. in Korea, '92-93; joined the Opposition led by Count Okuma. Vice-Minister of Agr. and Com. when the Matsukata Cabinet concluded an entente with the Progressives '96-97 and next Minister of the same Dep't. '98 in the Okuma-Itagaki coalition ministry; retired from political world in Jan. '15. Add. Nakano, near Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n873/mode/1up))

**Oka, Asajiro** D. Sch., Mem. of Imp. Academy, Prof. of Biology at the Tokyo Higher Normal School since '95; b. 1868 in Shizuoka-ken; grad. from Coll. Sc., Imp. Tokyo Univ., class '86 and further studied in Germany till '91. After some years at the Yamaguchi High School he was transferred to the present chair. Works: Lectures on Evolution, Evolution and Human Life, etc. Add. Tokyo Higher Normal School. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 874](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n874/mode/1up))

**Oka, Kishichiro** Crown Mem. House of Peers (nom. '14); b. in Okayama in 1866; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '91 and successively held the post of secretary in the provinces '93-04; Governor of Akita '04-05; Chief Police Commissioner at Korean Residency-Gen. in '05; and deputy Dir. of Agr. Ind. and Com. Dept. and of the Interior in Korea '07-10; Governor of Tottori '10-13; Dir. of Police Bureau, Home Office, '13-14; Sup't of Metropolitan Police, '18-21. Add. Hongo Shinhanacho, Tokyo. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 874](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n874/mode/1up))

**Oka, Minoru** D.L., former Dir. of Trade and Ind. Bur., Dep't of Agr. and Com.; b. in Nara-ken in 1873; grad. Politics, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '98; passed higher civil service exam. same year; Councillor of Legislation Bureau and then Sec. of the Dep't; Chief Commissioner at the Liege World Exh. in '95; attended various international economic and labor conferences; is director of the Tokyo Nichi-Nichi Shimbun. Add. Shirokane Sanko-cho, Tokyo. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 874](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n874/mode/1up))

**Okada, Bunji** Mem. House of Peers, Chairman Kokkatsu (International Film) Co.; b. 1874 in Yamagata-ken; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '08 and passed civil service exam. same year; was in service of provincial govts. before his app. to Gov. of Tochigi-ken then of Karafuto '14; Sup't of Metropolitan Police '16-18. Add. Aoyama Takagi-cho, Tokyo. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 874](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n874/mode/1up))

**Okada, Keisuke** Adm., Minister of Navy; b. 1868 in Fukui Pref.; grad. from the Navy Academy Apr. 1890; appointed Sub-Lieutenant, July '90; promoted to Lieut. '94, Lt.-Com. in '99, Commander in '04 and Captain in '[?]0, and at the same time Dir. of the Torpedo School; Rear-Adm. and Chief of Ordnance Dept. of Sasebo Arsenal, '12; Vice-Adm. in '16; and Chief of Sasebo Arsenal; Chief of the Fleet Administration Dept., '20-23; Vice-Minister of Navy in '23-24; full admiral '24; Supreme War Councillor June '24; Commander-in-Chief of 1st Squadron, and of Combined Squadron '24-26; Commander-in-Chief of Yokohama Naval Station Dec. '26; thrice made member of Admirals Council in '18, '23 and '26; present post in Apr. '27. Add. Navy Dept., Tokyo. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 874](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n874/mode/1up))

**Okada, Ryohei** Crown Mem. House of Peers; Minister of Education '24-27; b. 1864 in Shizuoka-ken; grad. from Literature, Imp. Tokyo University, in '87; was teacher of the Tokyo High School till '93; Councillor of Dept. of Education and next Director of Yamaguchi High School; Vice-Minister of Education '01-03; Deputy President of the Peers' School, '06-07; Presi- dent, Imp. Kyoto University '07; Vice-Minister of Education '08-11; Minister of same, '16-18 and again in '24 Add. Koishikawa Haramachi, Tokyo. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 874](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n874/mode/1up))

**Okada, Shin-ichiro** Prof. of the Tokyo Academy of Fine Arts, Lecturer in Architecture, Waseda Univ.; b. in Saitama-ken; grad. in engineering Tokyo Imp. Univ., 1906. He has designed the Kabukiza Theatre and many other buildings. Add. Ushigome, Kagura-cho, Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n875/mode/1up))

**Okada, Takematsu** D. Sch., F.R. Met. S. (England), Dir. Central Meteorological Observatory and of Oceanic Meteorological Observatory, Mem. of Govt. Earthquake Com. since '23; b. 1874 in Chiba-ken; grad. from Physics at the Imp. Tokyo Univ., class '90; represented Japan at the International Meteorological conference in '26. Add. Central Meteorological Observatory. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n875/mode/1up))

**Okada, Waichiro** M.D., Prof. of Otorhinolaryngology, Imp. Tokyo Univ., Pres. Otorhinolar. Society in Japan; b. 1864 in Ehime-ken; grad. from Coll. Med., of above institution, class '89; after serving a term of assist. at his alma mater was sent to Germany and Austria to study his speciality, '96-99; prof. at the alma mater since '02; attended the International Medical Conference in America in '22. Add. Sambancho, Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n875/mode/1up))

**Okakura, Yoshisaburo** Prof. at Tokyo Higher Normal School and an English scholar; b. in 1865; grad. at Philology, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '88; was sent to Korea to investigate Korean language; next to England to study method of teaching English '01-04; lecturer at the Lowell Institute, Boston '09. Works; "The Spirit of Japan", "The Life and Thought of Japan," etc. Add. Naka-arai, Nerima, near Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n875/mode/1up))

**Okamoto, Eitaro** Pres. of Cen. Bank for Coop. So. and ex-Vice-Min. Agr. and Com. Dept '24; b. 1871 in Osaka-fu; edu. in Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '98; entered the Dept. of Agr. and Com. to be promoted to dir. of various bureaus; once sec. to Anglo-Japanese Fair. Hyde Park; a trip to Europe '10. Add. Omori Araijuku, Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n875/mode/1up))

**Okamoto, Sakura** Pres. of Toho (Nagoya) Gas Co. & Dir. of the Tokyo Gas Co., etc.; b. April 1878 in Hyogo pref.; grad. from engineering college, Tokyo Imp. Univ.; is authority on gas industry. Add. Nagoya. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n875/mode/1up))

**Okamoto, Yanamatsu** D.M., Emeritus Prof. of the Medical Dept. of the Kyoto Imp. Univ.; b. 1863 in Hyogo-ken; grad. from the Medical Coll. of the Tokyo Imp. Univ., '89; further studied in Germany. Add. Yoshida-machi, Kami-ohji, Kyoto. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n875/mode/1up))

**Okamura, Kintaro** D. Sc., Specialist in Sea-weeds, Dir. of the Govt. Fishery Institute; b. 1864 in Tokyo. Edu. at the Coll. of Sc., Imp. Tokyo Univ. class '89. After having been Prof. at the Fourth High School for few years was appointed to the present post. Add. Gov. Fishery Institute, Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n875/mode/1up))

**Okamura, Tatsuhiko** M. D., Dermatologist, and Dir. of the Okamura Hospital; b. 1870 in Tokyo; Edu. at Coll. Med. Imp. Tokyo Univ., class '95. After serving a term of assist. at the alma mater, further studied his speciality at Freiburg and Vienna, '96-00. Established the present hospital in '00. Add. Kanda Nishi Ogawa-machi, Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n875/mode/1up))

**Okano, Noburo** Dr. Eng., Vice-Min. of Railways '24-25; b. 1876, brother to Keijiro Okano; edu. in Eng. at Imp. Tokyo Univ. and entered the service of Govt. Rlys. in which he was an Engineer and then Dir. of Way and Works. Add. Jujo, near Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n875/mode/1up))

**Okawa, Heizaburo** Pres. Fuji Paper Mill, etc.; b. 1861 in Tokyo; married daughter of Visc. Shibusawa. Made a tour of inspection in Europe and America several times. Add. Mukojima Kommecho, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 876](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n876/mode/1up))

**Okazaki, Kuniomi** Pres. of the Tokyo Stock Exchange since '25; b. in Shimane-ken, 1874; grad. at Politics, Tokyo Imp. Univ. '90; entered Dept. of Agr. and Com. and remained there for years; app. Chief Secretary of the House of Representatives; Mng.-Dir. of the Exchange for years. Add. Shimo-Osaki, near Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 876](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n876/mode/1up))

**Okazaki, Kunisuke** ex-Min. Agriculture and Forestry, M.P., Dir. of Keihan Electric Co., etc. Is more widely known as a politician who works behind the scene; was a confidant of the late Count Mutsu and was 1st Councillor of Communication Dep't. when the late Hoshi held the portfolio, but has only short Gov. career, having been once a Chief Police Inspector; was adviser to the Furukawa Mining Co. Add. Shiba Takanawa, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 876](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n876/mode/1up))

**Okazaki, Kyujiro** Pres. of the Americo-Japanese Co., Ltd., the Teikoku Storage Battery Co., Ltd., and the Dai Nippon Bicycles Co., Ltd., M. P.; b. 1874 in Yokohama; grad. from the Tokyo Higher Com. School; thrice elected M.P. Add. Shirokane Imazato-cho, Shiba-ku, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 876](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n876/mode/1up))

**Okochi, Masatoshi** Visc. (suc. '07), D. Eng., Prof. at Eng. Coll. Imp. Tokyo Univ. '04-25; Mem. of House of Peers; b. 1878 at Toyohashi; grad. from the said college '03; further studied in Germany and France '08-11; sent to Tsingtao to prosecute scientific investigation there during the 1914 War; Senior Secretary of Navy '20 and Head of Scientific Investigation Office since '21. Add. Uyeno Yanaka Shimizucho, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 876](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n876/mode/1up))

**Okonogi, Shinrokuro** Director of Okonogi Otological Hospital; b. 1860 in Fukushima-ken; first studied in Tokyo Imperial Univ., but soon left it to go over to Germany for study '88-94; established his own hospital in '96. Add. Hongo Motomachi, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 876](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n876/mode/1up))

**Oku, Yasukata** Marshal, Count (cr. Baron '95, Count 18[?]7), ex-Chief of General Staff and Mem. Supreme Military Council; b. 1846 in Fukuoka-ken; entered the Army '71; won distinction in the Civil War of '77 for having cut through the besieging lines of the rebels round Kumamoto; commanded the 5th Army Division (Hiroshima) in the Japan-China War; Com. of the Tokyo Bay Defence, Com. of the Eastern Military Districts, and held other high posts; promoted to full Gen. '03; attended the Durbar at Delhi; led the Second Army in Russo-Japanese War; Chief of the General Staff '06-12; Marshal in '11. Add. Ushigome Haraikatamachi, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 876](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n876/mode/1up))

**Okubo, Toshikazu** Marquis; b. 1859 in Kagoshima; eldest son of Toshimichi, one of the builders of the work of Restoration who was assassinated in '78. The present head was once a Junior Secretary of the Treasury and is Mem. of House of Peers. Add. Shiba Nihon-[illegible], Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 876](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n876/mode/1up))

**Okubo, Toshitake** Crown Mem. House of Peers; b. 1866, in Kagoshima, brother of the above and Count Makino; studied in U.S.A. and Germany '87-95; Secretary at Formosan Govt.-Gen. '95-96; Dir. of Prison Affairs Bureau '99; local Governor for years. Add. Yotsuya Minami-iga-cho, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 876](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n876/mode/1up))

**Okubo, Toshitaka** ex-Manager of the London Branch, Yokohama Specie Bank; brother of the other two Okubos; b. in 1878; grad. at Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '03; and entered the Bank. Add. Shiba Shirokane-Daimachi, Tokyo. — p. 82 ([scan leaf 877](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n877/mode/1up))

**Okuda, Takematsu** ex-Manager of Shirokiya Drygoods Store, ex-Counsellor at Japanese Embassy in Berlin; b. 1873 in Okayama; graduated from Keio University; passed the Diplomatic and Consular service exam. '95, and in diplomatic service but resigned in 1916. Add. Akasaka Nakanocho, Tokyo. — p. 82 ([scan leaf 877](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n877/mode/1up))

**Okudaira, Masayasu** Count, formerly of the daimiate House of the fief of Buzen, Mem. House of Peers; b. in 1877 in Tokyo; graduated from the Peers' School; further studied at Kyoto Imperial University; is connected with Takasago Life Ins. Co. Add. Shiba Takanawa, Tokyo. — p. 82 ([scan leaf 877](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n877/mode/1up))

**Okuma, Nobutsune** Marquis (2nd), adopted son and heir of the late Shigenobu Okuma; b. in Tokyo 1870, 3rd son of the late Count Matsu-ura; grad. from Peers School in '93 and then Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '97; at Cambridge '05-07; Personal Secy. to Premier '14-16; M.P. '15-16, and Parl. Jun. Secy. to Edu. Dept. July '15, then to Foreign Dept. Oct. '15. Add. Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 82 ([scan leaf 877](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n877/mode/1up))

**Okuma, Ujihiro** sculptor; b. 1856 in Saitama; grad. from the Art. Department of the former Engineering Coll. in '80; studied in Italy '95-99; cast bronze statues of Omura, Princes Kitashirakawa and Arisugawa, Prince Ito, etc.; has won gold or silver medals at home and abroad. Add. Koishikawa Takehayacho, Tokyo. — p. 82 ([scan leaf 877](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n877/mode/1up))

**Okura, Kihachiro** first Baron (cr. '16) one of the new-made millionaires, and founder of Okuragumi, Ltd., Okura Business School, etc.; b. 1837 in Echigo; removed to Tokyo early in the Meiji era, and started business of selling arms and ammunition, from which he derived a big profit; created present firm in '73 and opened a branch store in London '74; travelled twice in Europe and America in '72 and '00, and in Mongolia in '25 when 88 years old. Resigned the Barony in favour of his son in '26; decorated in '28 with 1st order of Rising Sun (Grand Cordon) for his services rendered to the State. His famous art collection was destroyed in 1912. Add. Akasaka Aoicho, Tokyo. — p. 82 ([scan leaf 877](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n877/mode/1up))

**Okura, Kimmochi** Baron, Dir. of the South Man. R'ly. since '22; 3rd son and heir of the late Gen. Okura; grad. from the Civil Eng., Imp. Tokyo Univ., '04; Eng. in the Rly Dept; Vice-Chief of the Transportation Dept. the S.M.R. Add. Dairen. — p. 82 ([scan leaf 877](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n877/mode/1up))

**Okura, Kishichiro** Baron (succeeded '26), Pres. of Okura Mining Co., Dir. of Okura Trading Co., Akita Timber Mfg. Co., Rly. Car. Mfg. Co., etc.; b. 1882; first son of Kihachiro; studied at Cambridge and at other institutions in London; returned home '06 to devote himself to his father's business. Add. Azabu Hiro-o, Tokyo. — p. 82 ([scan leaf 877](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n877/mode/1up))

**Okuyama, Seiji** former Minister to Greece ('25-26); b. 1876 in Yamagata-ken; edu. at Imp. Tokyo Univ., in Law, class '03; passed the dip. and con. service exam. soon after and entered the Foreign Office first as eleve consul, then 3rd Legation Sec., Counsellor at the head office; Minister to Athens, '25-26. Add. Foreign Office. — p. 82 ([scan leaf 877](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n877/mode/1up))

**Omura, Hikotaro** formerly prop. of Shirokiya Dry-goods Store and now Pres. of the same reorganized in '18 into a Ltd. Co.; b. 1869 in Kyoto; edu. at Tokyo Com. School and Cheltenham Coll., England. Is Mem. of Tokyo Chamber of Commerce. Add. Nihombashi, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 878](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n878/mode/1up))

**Ono, Giichi** M.P.; b. 1876 in Kochi-ken; grad. at Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '03; entered the Finance Dept. in which he held the post of Custom House Chief at Kobe, etc.; Vice-Minister '24. Add. Azabu Mikawadai, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 878](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n878/mode/1up))

**Ono, Tetsuro** Chairman, Nippon Raw Silk Co., Dir., Ono Trading Co., Yokohama Fire & Marine Ins.; b. 1890 in Yokohama, studied economics at Keio; further studied in U.S.; is engaged in silk trade. Add. Tsukioka-cho, Yokohama. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 878](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n878/mode/1up))

**Onoye, Baiko** (real name Einosuke Terajima) a distinguished "male actress," b. 1871 in Nagoya; adopted by the late 5th Kikugoro Onoye, famous actor; began his stage career under the guidance of the late Danjuro Ichikawa. Add. Sendagaya, near Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 878](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n878/mode/1up))

**Onoye, Hachiro** Litt. D., master writer of the Mannyo style of script, Prof. at Tokyo Higher Womens School and other institutions; b. in Tsuyama in 1876; edu. at Imp. Tokyo Univ. in Literature, class 1900. Works; poetical works, History of Japanese Literature. Add. Koishikawa Haramachi, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 878](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n878/mode/1up))

**Onoye, Kikugoro** (real name Kozo Terajima), distinguished Tokyo actor; b. 1885 in Tokyo and is the 6th of the hereditary stage name. Add. Shiba Park, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 878](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n878/mode/1up))

**Onozuka, Kiheiji** D.C.L., Prof. of Tokyo Imp. Univ., Mem. House of Peers, Mem. Imp. Academy; b. 1871 in Niigata-ken; grad. from Law Coll. Tokyo Imp. Univ., '95; further studied politics at the Universities of Heidelberg and Berlin and then at the Paris Political College, '97-'01. Add. Koishikawa Kobinatadaimachi, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 878](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n878/mode/1up))

**O-oka, Ikuzo** M.P. b. 1856 in Yamaguchi-ken; was long a lawyer in Tokyo which business he has subsequently left to devote himself to politics; was once a member of the National Unionist Association; Chairman, Tokyo City Council; Pres. of House of Rep. in '12-15, and again in '17; Minister of Education in March-April '14. Add. Shiba Matsumoto-cho, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 878](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n878/mode/1up))

**Oshima, Hisanao** Visc. (cr. '07), Gen. (ret.); b. 1848 at Akita; Lieut. '71; Major '74 and took part in the Satsuma Civil War; Lieut.-Col. '81; Maj.-Gen. '92; commanded a Brigade in the Japan-China War and was rewarded with Baronage; Lieut.-Gen. '98; his Division formed part of Gen. Nogi's first investing Army at Port Arthur and next the out-flanking corps at Mukden. Promoted to full General, May '06; Commander of the Body Guards '07; transferred to Chief of Military Edu. Board and then Mem. of Sup. Council of War; on reserve list in '13. Add. Shimo-Ochiai, near Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 878](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n878/mode/1up))

**Oshima, Kenichi** Lieut.-Gen. (ret.
