'19), Crown Mem. of House of Peers; b. in 1858; Sub-Lieut. of Artillery in '81; studied in Germany and France '90-93; was on Marshal Yamagata's suite to attend the Coronation Ceremony of the Czar in '96 and also on the suite of Prince Kan-in to Europe on his official mission in '00; Major-Gen. in '07; Vice-Chief of the General Staff Board, '12-14; Vice-Min. of War. '14 and full Minister, '16-18.; Com.-in-Chief Tsingtao Garrison '18-20. Add. Ushigome Nakacho, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 878](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n878/mode/1up))

**Oshima, Kintaro** D. Agr., Prof. Imp. Hokkaido Univ., Dir. of Formosan Central Research Institute; b. 1871 in Nagano-ken; grad. from Sapporo Agr. Coll. '93; appointed Assist. Prof. of alma mater '95; sent to Germany and then to America to study agr. chemistry, '98-03 at Halle, Gottingen and Berlin, and Wesleyan Univ. U.S.A. Add. Sapporo, Hokkaido. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 879](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n879/mode/1up))

**Oshima, Gishu** Dir. of the Imp. Museum, Ueno, since '24; b. in Tokyo, 1871; grad., in Philosophy, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '94; successively filled the posts of Educational Inspector, Dir. of the Tokyo Music Academy, Dir. of the 8th High School, etc., Dir. of Peeress' School '20-24. Add. Azabu Hiro-o, Tokyo. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 879](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n879/mode/1up))

**Osumi, Mineo** Vice-Adm., Perm. Vice-Min. Navy; b. 1876 in Aichi-ken; graduated from the Naval Academy, '98; an hero of the memorable Port Arthur blockade in the Russo-Japanese War; steadily promoted and by '20 he was a Rear-Adm. and a senior staff officer of the General Staff, meanwhile having been Adjutant to the Minister, Commander of the Asahi, Naval Attache in France, etc; attended the Washington Disarmament Conference as assis. to the chief naval delegate; also the second League of Nations Conference; Vice-Adm. and present post in '23. Add. Navy Dept., Tokyo. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 879](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n879/mode/1up))

**Ota, Masahiro** Superintendent of the Metropolitan Police '24-27; b. 1870 in Yamagata-ken; grad. from Law Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ., class 1896; was Chief of the 1st Dept. of the Metropolitan Police Board; Governor of Fukushima, Ishikawa, Kumamoto, Niigata and Aichi Prefectures successively; chief of Metropolitan Police in June '24. Add. Tokyo. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 879](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n879/mode/1up))

**Ota, Mitsuhiro** President of the Miye Electric Co., and of the Kei-Han Electric Railway Co., Dir. of Daido Electric Power Co., Shin Kei-Han Railway Co.; b. 1874 in Yamaguchi; adopted son of Kosaburo Ota; grad. from Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ. '98; Secretary of the Imperial Railway Board; entered the Kei-Han Electric Co., Ltd., as its Mng. Dir., in '06. Add. Kei-Han Office, Osaka. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 879](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n879/mode/1up))

**Ota, Tamekichi** Minister to Spain and Portugal; b. 1880 in Tottori-ken; passed Higher Civil Service Exam. and entered Foreign Office, '03; passed Dip. Service Exam. and Eleve-Con. '07; Secretary to the Kwantung Govt., Consul at various places; Legation Secretary and Charge d'Affaires at Mexico, then Consul-Gen. at San Francisco, '18-20; Consul-Gen. in Ottawa '21-23; Legation Counsellor in Peking '23-26; present post '27. Add. Jap. Legation, Madrid. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 879](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n879/mode/1up))

**Otani, Kahei** tea and silk exporter at Yokohama, Chairman of the Japan Tea Guild and of Yokohama Chamber of Commerce and Pres. of the 7th Bank, Aud. of Teikoku Marine, Transport, Fire Ins. Co., Japan Hypothec Bank and Bank of Formosa, etc.; b. 1844 in Miye-ken; had long been a clerk in a foreign firm in Yokohama dealing in tea; next set up a tea store on his own account. Add. Motohama-cho, Yokohama. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 879](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n879/mode/1up))

**Otani, Kozui** Count, ex-Lord Abbot of the Western Hon- gwanji Temple, one of the two greatest Buddhist centres in Japan; b. at Kyoto 1876; studied long in Europe, and started for home '02 via Central Asia, but was obliged to hurry home before he could carry out his program on learning of the death of his father; visited Saghalien and China '07; and also India '10. Retiring from active service '14, again visited Buddha land via Korea, China and Burmah, and is now staying in China. Add. Shanghai. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 879](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n879/mode/1up))

**Otani, Noboru** Mng.-Dir. of Nippon Yusen Kaisha; b. 1870 in Fukui-ken; edu. at the Tokyo Account School, class '90; entered the present Co.; Agent at Calcutta, '20-22; present post '2[?]. Add. Kami-Osaki, near Tokyo. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 880](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n880/mode/1up))

**Otori, Fujitaro** 2nd Baron. Mem. House of Peers; b. 1866 in Tokyo, eldest son of the late Baron Keisuuke Otori; grad. at Law Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ. '90; in service of Formosan Govt. until 1901, when he passed Dip. and Con. Service Exam.; Attache to Legation at Seoul same year; Secy. of Legation at The Hague '03, and Brussels '05; Peace Commissioner at The Hague '07; Con.-Gen. Vladivostok '09; Secy. Foreign Office '12; Embassy Counsellor at Rome '14; at Paris '16-19; Min. to Mexico '19-20. Add. Azabu Mikawadai, Tokyo. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 880](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n880/mode/1up))

**Otsuka, Yasuji** D. Litt., Prof. Tokyo Imp. Univ.; b. 1868 in Gumma-ken; grad. from Lit. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ.; further studied in Germany, France and Italy '96-00. Add. Ushigome Ichigaya Sadowara-cho, Tokyo. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 880](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n880/mode/1up))

**Otsuki, Fumihiko** Litt. Dr., famous scholar of Japanese classics, Mem. of the Imperial Academy; b. at Sendai in 1847; studied at the Shogunate Academy (Shohei-ko), and was afterwards attached to the Education Department; has written "Japanese Dictionary," "Japanese Grammar," etc. Add. Nippori, near Tokyo. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 880](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n880/mode/1up))

**Ouchi, Hyoe** Man. of Ohara Institute for Research in Social Problems, Assist. Prof. at Imp. Tokyo Univ., his alma mater which post he had once to resign for having inserted an article on Kropotkin in a monthly organ of the Univ. Economic Dept. of which he was a nominal editor; restored to the chair when he returned home from his two years' sojourn abroad. Works; Conflict between Labor and Capital in British Parliament. Add. Okubo Hyakunin-machi, Tokyo. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 880](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n880/mode/1up))

**Oya, Gompei** D. Eng., ex-Dir. of Chosen Govt. Railway Bureau; b. 1862 in Yamaguchi; grad. Eng. Dep't., Imp. Tokyo Univ. '81; railway engineer in '94; despatched to Europe and America to investigate railway business in '01; for a short time on retired list in '03, re-installed in a post in Chosen Govt. Railway in '06. Add. Kami-Osaki, near Tokyo. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 880](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n880/mode/1up))

**Oyama, Atsushi** ex-M.P., lawyer, Lord-in-Waiting of Kinkei Hall; b. 1865 in Nagoya; grad. at Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '89; in juridical service either as Judge or Public Procurator till '04; went abroad for purpose of study, returning home in '06; reinstalled in the former service; Dir. Prison Bureau '07-12; Vice-Min. of Justice '12-14. Began practice in '16. Add. Koishikawa Haramachi, Tokyo. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 880](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n880/mode/1up))

**Oyama, Ikuo** former Prof. Waseda University which he graduated in Politics and Economy, class '05; b. 1880 in Hyogo-ken; sent by the Univ. to England for study and stayed there four years; on the editorial staff of the Osaka Asahi in '17 which he resigned in '18 and founded with his friends a monthly "We". Works; Democratic Culture, Social Foundation of Politics, Social Education, etc. Add. Toyamagahara, Tokyo. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 880](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n880/mode/1up))

**Oyama, Kashiwa** 2nd Prince; b. 1889, son to the late Marshal Prince Oyama, Com.-in-Chief of the Imp. Army during the Russo-Japanese War; grad. Military Coll. '09; Sub-Lt. '10; Lt. '13; Capt. '18; married Prince Konoye's sister; toured abroad '23. Archaeology is his hobby. Add. Harajuku, Tokyo. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 881](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n881/mode/1up))

**Ozaki, Yukio** M.P. of unbroken record, ex-Minister of Justice; b. 1859 in Miye-ken; studied at Keio, and for a short while held post of Jun. Secretary at Central Govt., but soon became a cause champion of the clan Govt. for introducing party politics; was ordered to leave Tokyo by the coup d'etat in '87 and went to England, returning home on the eve of the summoning of the 1st session of the Diet to sit in it ever since. He was in '97 Chief Counsellor of the Foreign Office in the Matsukata Progressive Cabinet; Minister of Education in '98; resigned the post and severed connection with Count Okuma's party; joined Prince Ito's new party, left it in '03 but to rejoin in '10; Mayor of Tokyo '13-14; Min. of Justice '14-16; joined hand with the Kenseikai Party in '21 but left it soon after; is Adviser to the Kakushin-to Party. Married Miss Theodora, d. of Baron Osaki by English lady. Add. Shinagawa, Tokyo. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 881](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n881/mode/1up))

**Rinoie, Ryusuke** Mayor of Shimonoseki since '20, ex-Gov. of Nagasaki-ken, b. 1866 at Yamaguchi; grad. Imp. Tokyo Univ. '90; held junior post in the Cabinet; then Home Dept.; Councillor and Secretary, and Gov. of various prefectures till '17. Add. Shimonoseki. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 881](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n881/mode/1up))

**Saburi, Sadao** Embassy Counsellor in London since '27; b. 1879 in Tokyo; brother of Dr. Isshi Saburi; grad. from Imp. Tokyo Univ., class '05; after passing the Dip. and Con. Service Exam. was appointed Attache in China, '05; in Russia, '06; then in France; at Head Office, '08; Sec. in France, 12; at Head Office, '18; Emb. Sec. and then Counsellor at Washington till '24; Dir. of Commercial Bureau '26; Dir. of Treaty Bureau '27; attended the Chinese Tariff Revision Conference '26-27. Add. Jap. Embassy, London. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 881](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n881/mode/1up))

**Sahara, Tokusuke** Shanghai Correspondent of the Jiji, Asahi, and Mainichi; Dir. of the Shanghai Mercury, Ltd.; b. 1874 in Tokyo; educated at the Keio Univ.; on graduation joined the editorial staff of the Jiji Shimpo; despatched to Shanghai as its special correspondent in '99; became Sub-Editor of Shanghai Mercury (English daily) in '04; Dir. of the Shanghai Mercury, Ltd.; '17; is a member of the Inst. of Journalists in England since '07. Add. Miller Road, Shanghai. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 881](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n881/mode/1up))

**Saigo, Yorinori** 2nd Marquis, Col. Infantry, b. 187[?] son of the late Saigo Yorimichi (d. 19[?]2), one of the notables associated with the Restoration. Add. Kamimeguro, near Tokyo. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 881](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n881/mode/1up))

**Saiki, Nori** M.D., Ph. D. and M. Sc. (Yale), Dietist and Dir. of the Dietic Laboratory, Home Office; b. 1876 in Ehime-ken; grad. from the Med. Dep., Third High School, class '98; further studied at Yale '06-08. Served as Assis. at the alma mater, '98-99; assisted his father's practice in the country. '99-01; assist. in the Med. Chemico Class-room, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '01- '02; in the Infectious Disease Institute, Home Office, '02. Add. Gov. Dietic Laboratory, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 881](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n881/mode/1up))

**Saionji, Hachiro** Chief of Imperial Mews; b. 1881 of the Mori family, a former Daimyo, and was adopted as heir by the following; once Private Secretary to his father-in-law when Premier; also accompanied him as Peace Envoy to Paris '18; was on the Crown Prince's suite when he visited Europe in '21; Vice-Grand Master of Ceremonies, '24-27. Add. Azabu Higura-katamachi, Tokyo. — p. 87 ([scan leaf 882](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n882/mode/1up))

**Saionji, Kimmochi** Prince (Marquis '84, Prince '22), formerly courtier), ex-Premier and ex-Leader of Seiyukai; b. 18[?]9 at Kyoto and adopted as heir by the house; took part when under 20 in the grave councils of the Restoration; at 19 he was Commander-in-Chief of an Imperial Army; Gov. of Niigata-ken for a few months in '68; studied in France '69-'80, and brought home highly democratic notions issuing at one time a democratic paper. Then commenced his official career, first as Vice-Senator, '81; accompanied Prince (then Mr.) Ito to Europe and U.S.A. to investigate Parliamentary system, '82; Senator '83; Minister to Austria '85; then to Berlin, '88; President of the Board of Decoration, '91; Vice-Pres. Code Investigation Commission and Vice-Pres. House of Peers, '93; Privy Councillor, '94; held portfolio of Education in the 2nd Ito Cabinet '94-96, temporarily acting as Min. of Foreign Affairs; Min. of Education in the 3rd Ito Cabinet and President of the Privy Council and as a President of the Privy Council and as a President he had to occupy post of Prime Minister ad interim no less than three times during the intervals between the resignation of one Ministry and the formation of another; became leader of the Seiyukai, '03 and twice formed his own Cabinet in '06-07 and '11-12; represented Japan as Peace Envoy at Versailles '1[?] and was invested with the Grand Order of Merit. He is brother to the late Prince Tokudaiji and Baron Kichizaemon Sumitomo (died '26). Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 87 ([scan leaf 882](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n882/mode/1up))

**Saito, Hidesaburo** educationist and English scholar, Pres. of the Seisoku English Language School; b. 1866 in Miyagi-ken; edu. first at the Kobu Daigaku about five years, but left it unfinished; instructor at the 1st Higher Sch. '88 and the Kokumin Eigakkai; founded the present school in '96. Works; a number of text books on English grammar, an English-Japanese dictionary and other works. Add. Kojimachi Gobancho, Tokyo. — p. 87 ([scan leaf 882](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n882/mode/1up))

**Saito, Hiroshi** Con-Gen. at New York since '23; b. 1886 in Tokyo. Grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., class '10 and passed the Dip. and Con. Exam. and also the Higher Civil Ser. the same year; Attache in America, '10; 3rd Emb. Sec., '16 in England, and appointed Assis. to the Peace Envoy, '19; 2nd Sec. '19; Con. at Seattle, '21; attended as assistant commissioner international conferences in Europe and America. Add. New York. — p. 87 ([scan leaf 882](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n882/mode/1up))

**Saito, Minoru** Viscount (Baron '07, Visc. '25), Adm. (ret), Privy Councillor since Dec. '27; b. 1858 in Iwate-ken; entered Navy '73; at the time of Sino-Japanese War he held post of naval aide-de-camp to His Majesty; Com. '97 and Capt. '98; Vice-Minister of the Navy under Adm. Count Yamamoto same year; Rear-Adm. '00; Vice-Adm. '04; full Adm. in '12; granted 2nd class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun for his service in connection with the Russo-Japanese War; Minister of the Navy '13-14; Gov.-Gen. of Chosen, '19-27; represented Japan in the 2nd Naval Armament Limitation Conference at Geneva, '27; present post, Dec. '27. Add. Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 87 ([scan leaf 882](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n882/mode/1up))

**Saito, Ryoei** D.L., Dir. of South Manchuria Rly. Co.; b. 1880 in Fukushima-ken. Edu. at Imp. Tokyo Univ., politics, class '08; passed the Dip. and Con. Exam., '10; Eleve-Con. in China, '10; Ottawa, '14; Con. at Fuchow, '15; Sec. at Head Office, '17; Jr. Commissioner at the 1st congress of League of Nations at Geneva; 2nd Emb. Sec. at Washington in '20; Sec. at Head Office, '22; Dir. of Commercial Bureau '27; present post in July '27. Add. Dairen. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 883](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n883/mode/1up))

**Saito, Tsunezo** D. Eng., Chairman of the Spinners Union, Pres. of the Toyo Cotton Mill, Ltd.; b. at Hagi in 1858. Edu. at the Govt. Eng. Coll. which was later merged with the present Imp. Tokyo Univ.; was for some years an official of the Osaka Mint; entered the then Miye Mill expanded to the present Mill by absorbing several other minor mills being connected with it a little under 40 years. His inspection of the American cotton industry fired his zeal in this business. Has been awarded with the Blue Ribbon (public utility) Medal. Add. Toyo Cotton Mill, Osaka. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 883](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n883/mode/1up))

**Saito, Tsutomu** Man. Nippon Chuya Bank, Dir. Japan Club; b. 1880 in Tokyo-fu; grad. from Law Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ. '07; and entering Yokohama Specie Bank served at the New York and then, London, branch; Manager of Japan Industrial Bank, '15. Add. Ushigome Kagacho, Tokyo. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 883](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n883/mode/1up))

**Saka, Nakasuke** Chairman, Board of Dirs. Fujita Mining, Fujita Gumi, etc; b. 1879 in Yamaguchi-ken; passed higher civil service examination '95 and entered the Home Dep't. in '01 promoted to Personal Secy. of the Home Minister; transferred to local government '04-08; Governor of Ishikawa, etc; entered Sumitomo Firm, '16. Add. Nizaimon-cho, Osaka. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 883](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n883/mode/1up))

**Sakai, Tokutaro** Mng. of Mitsui & Co., Chairman of the Harvard Club; b. 1880 in Nagoya; grad. from the Rikkyo Gakko (St. Pauls School) '91; Harvard, '94, and its University Hall, '97. Toured abroad a number of times, staying with Visc. Kaneko in U.S.A. during the Russo-Japanese war, visiting England in '07 in connection with the Anglo-Japanese Exhibition, and accompanying Visc. Oura, then Min. of Agr. and Com., on his foreign trip in '10. Was Pers. Secretary to the late Marquis Komura as Foreign Minister, '09; retired from official life and entered the Co. Add. Shibuya-machi, Tokyo. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 883](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n883/mode/1up))

**Sakai, Toshihiko** (nom de plume Kosen), socialist; b. 1870 in Fukuoka-ken; studied at Tokyo Higher School; started in life as a newspaper man; published for some time a socialistic organ; rose to prominence as a socialistic pacifist during the Russo-Japanese War; was imprisoned on charge of disturbing social order on several occasions. Several translations and original works. Add. Koj. Motozono-cho, Tokyo. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 883](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n883/mode/1up))

**Sakaki, Junjiro** M.D., Dir. and Prop. of Sakaki Hospital for Women's Diseases, Tokyo; b. in Tokyo 1859; grad. Med. Imp. Tokyo Univ., then studied his speciality at Berlin. Add. Koj. Sambancho, Tokyo. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 883](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n883/mode/1up))

**Sakata, Minoru** Pres. 1st Fire, Marine Re-ins. Co., etc.; b. 1857 in Okayama-ken; grad. from Keio. '76; Lecturer of the name; Principal of Okayama Mid. Sch. and Okayama Normal Sch., '83-86; was for a time on the editorial staff of the Jiji; entered the Bank of Japan, '99; Man. of its Nagoya Branch '00; promoted the Toyokuni Bank. Add. Azabu Nagata-cho, Tokyo. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 883](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n883/mode/1up))

**Sakatani, Yoshiro** Baron (cr. '07), D.L., ex-Min. of Finance and ex-Mayor of Tokyo; b. 1863 at Okayama, son of a celebrated scholar in Chinese classics. Grad. from the Govt. Univ., Tokyo, and entered the Dep't of Finance '87, with which he was long connected without a break, having been successively Counsellor, Accountant, Director of Accountant Bureau, Vice-Min. '01, full Minister '06-08; toured abroad '00; Vice-President of Census Com. '10; attended the Peace Conference at The Hague '11; Mayor of Tokyo in '12-15; despatched to Paris in '16 to represent Japan at the Financial Conference of the Allied Powers. Add. Koishikawa Hara-machi, Tokyo. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n884/mode/1up))

**Sakenobu, Nobumichi** ex-Minister to Chile ('24-28); b. in Akita-ken '78; edu. at Imp. Tokyo Univ. in Law, class '04 and soon passed the Dip. and Con. Service Exam.; Eleve-Con. at Seoul, Hongkong, etc.; Counsellor at the head office, '08; 3rd Emb. Sec. at Berlin, '10; 2nd Sec., '12; Con.-Gen. at Calcutta, '17; Emb. Counsellor in Russia, '21; Personnel Affairs Chief at the head office, 21. Add. Foreign Office, Tokyo. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n884/mode/1up))

**Sakikawa, Saishiro** Dir. of Patent Bureau since '23; b. 1869; grad. at Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '96; '17-18 entered Dept. Agr. and Com. in which he rose to be transferred to the present post. Dir. Mining Bureau. Add. Osaki, near Tokyo. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n884/mode/1up))

**Sakurai, Joji** D. Sc., LL.D., Privy Councillor, Pres. Imp. Acad., Pres. Nation. Res. Council, Emerit. Prof. of Chem. in Tokyo Imp. Univ., Hon. Mem. of Chem. Soc. of France, Soc. of Chem. Ind. of London, Roy. Inst. of Great Britain, Amer. Chem. Soc., and Acad. of Sci. of USSR; born in Kanazawa 1858; studied Chemistry in Univ. Coll. London '76-81; delegate to 9th Jubilee of Glasgow Univ. '01, Intern. Conf. on Intern. Catalogue of Sci. Liter. '07, Gen. Meet. of Intern. Assoc. of Acad. '10. Intern. Conf. of Sci. Acad. 18, Gen. Meet. of Intern. Res. Council '22, and Second Pan-Pacific Sci. Congress '23; Pres. Third Pan-Pacific Sci. Congress '26. Add. Hongo Akebonocho, Tokyo. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n884/mode/1up))

**Sakurai, Kotaro** D. Eng. (Architecture), Consulting Architect to the Mitsubishi Firm for whom he has designed a large number of buildings; b. 1870 in Tokyo; edu. at Imp. Tokyo Univ. in Architecture, class '95. Add. Ichigaya Nakano-cho, Tokyo. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n884/mode/1up))

**Samejima, Takenosuke** Mem. House of Peers since '96 and Aud. Bank of Japan; b. 1858 in Kagoshima; studied in U.S.A. while young and was appointed a teacher at the Foreign Language School on his return home; clerk to the Foreign Office about '82; Pers. Sec. to the Foreign Minister Count Okuma, next to the Premier, late Prince Ito, lastly Chief Sec. to the Cabinet. Add. Azabu Kogai-cho, Tokyo. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n884/mode/1up))

**Sanjo, Sanenori** 3rd Prince, formerly one of the seven seika (next to gosekke for which see Ichijo), House of Fujiwara, founded by Saneyuki (1080-1160 A.D.). The 2nd Prince died in '14, whose father Saneyoshi (1837-91) was a builder of the grand work of the Restoration; the present head b. '02. His aunt is Princess Kan-in. Add. Azabu Torii-zaka, Tokyo. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n884/mode/1up))

**Sano, Gaku** a socialist and ex-lecturer at Waseda; fled to Russia in '23 being implicated in a "Communist" movement; b. 1892 in Oita-ken; edu. at Imp. Tokyo Univ. in Politics, class '17; further studied at the Univ. Hall Agral Politics, Sociology, etc. Works: Marx or Kant (translation), Investigation of Russian Economy (vol. 1), etc. Add. Moscow. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n884/mode/1up))

**Sano, Ushijiro** D.L., Prof. at Imp. Tohoku (Sendai) Univ., his alma mater, having graduated from it in Politics, class '03; b. 1877 at Tsuruoka; also studied in Europe and America '08-12, and has since been connected with the University. Works; Interpretation of the Law of Election of the House of Representatives, Politics (vol. 1), M. P's. and Election, etc. Add. Sendai. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 885](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n885/mode/1up))

**Sano, Zensaku** D.L., Dir. and Prof. of Tokyo Univ. of Commerce; b. 1873 in Tokyo; grad. from the above in '94; proceeded twice to Europe and America, and studied further in England, Belgium, Germany, etc; represented Japan in the Pacific Conference held at Honolulu, 1922. Add. Sendagaya, near Tokyo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 885](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n885/mode/1up))

**Sasagawa, Taneo** (nom de plume Rimpu), Commissioner of the Home Office, Dir. of the Narita Mid. and Girls Schools, historian and essayist; b. 1870 in Tokyo; edu. at Imp. Tokyo Univ. in History, class '95; has been connected with Prince Mori's Historiographical Office, Sansho-do's Encyclopaedia Japonica, Meiji Senshu and other private universities; is also known as art critic. Add. Hongo Nishi Katamachi, Tokyo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 885](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n885/mode/1up))

**Sasaki, Chujiro** Zoologist and sericultural expert, D. Sc., ex-Prof. in the Agr. Coll. of the Imp. Tokyo Univ.: b. 1859 in Tokyo; grad. Science Coll. of the Univ. '80; Assist-Prof. in the present Coll. '82; Prof. in the 1st High School, etc. Add. Aoyama Minami-cho, Tokyo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 885](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n885/mode/1up))

**Sasaki, Masakichi** M.D., Adviser to the Sasaki Hospital, Surugadai, Tokyo; b. 1856. grad. Med. Coll. Imp. Tokyo Univ., '78; further studied in Germany '79-83; appointed on his return Prof. at his alma mater; Dean of the College of Medicine; but soon resigned the post to devote himself to practice. Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 885](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n885/mode/1up))

**Sasaki, Nobutsuna** Litt. D., poet, Mem. of Imperial Academy, Lect. at Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. in 1872 in Miye-ken; grad. from Lit. Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '88. Has written a Japanese anthology of poems. Add. Komagome Nishikatamachi, Tokyo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 885](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n885/mode/1up))

**Sasaki, Soichi** D.L., Prof. at Imp. Kyoto Univ. his alma mater from which he was graduated in Law, class '03; b. in Tottori in 1878; studied also in Europe '09-12. Works; Administration, Constitutional and Unconstitutional, etc. Add. Kyoto Univ. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 885](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n885/mode/1up))

**Sasaki, Taka-oki** (or Ryuko), M.D., Mem. of German Chemical Society and of Berlin Physiological Society, Pres. of Kyo-un-do (private) Hospital at Surugadai, Tokyo; b. 1878 in Tokyo; edu. at Coll. Med., Imp. Tokyo Univ., class '02, and further studied at the Post-grad. course and at Strasburg, Berlin, etc., staying there '05-10. Filled chair of Internal Medicine, '13-16, but resigning the post has devoted his service to the Hospital. Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 885](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n885/mode/1up))

**Sassa, Rempei** M.D., Chief of the Heart and Kidney Dep't, Kyo-undo (private) Hospital, Tokyo; b. 1882 in Okayama-ken; took license in '00; grad. from Med. Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ., '90; assistant to Dr. Kumakawa, his teacher '00-11; stud- ied at Munchen, Vienna and Halle, '11-14. Add. Kyo-undo Hospital, Tokyo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 885](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n885/mode/1up))

**Sata, Yashihiko** Dr. Med. (Japan), Pathologist & Pathological Anatomist, Pres. Osaka Pref. Medical College; b. 1871 in Kagoshima; grad. from the Med. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ. 1890; further studied in Germany, '97-00; appointed on the staff of the Osaka Medical School, '94 and then its director, '02; Lecturer at the Med. Coll., Kyoto Imp. Univ. as additional post, '04; toured through Europe and America by order of the Government, '12-13; appointed Dir. and Prof. of the Osaka Pref. Med. Coll. (University status) and Pres. of the University Hospital, '19; represented Japan at the International Tuberculosis Conference held in Naples in 1900 and was recommended its honorary president. Add. Dojima, Kitaku, Osaka. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 886](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n886/mode/1up))

**Sata, Yoshihisa** M.D., Dir. of Sata Nerve Hospital (private): b. 188[?] in Kagoshima. Edu. at Chiba Medical School, '11; served at the Yamada Nerve Hospital in Tokyo till '23; set up his own hospital. Granted the degree in '23 by the Imp. Tokyo Univ., by submitting a thesis. Add. Shiba Nishinokubo, Tokyo. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 886](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n886/mode/1up))

**Sataka, Sango** D.L., Mem. House of Peers since '24; b. 188[?] at Ogaki; grad. from German Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ.; class, '05; Counsellor of the Legislative Bureau, '06; Office of the South Manchuria R'ly. Co. '8[?]; Counsellor of the Imp. R'lys., '12 and was connected with it till '18, rising to a Bureau Director; Mng. Dir. of a Private Rly. Co. in Osaka till '24, then Dir. of the Legislative Bureau. From '08 till '11 he studied state railway problems mostly in Germany. Add. Koishikawa Kago-machi, Tokyo. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 886](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n886/mode/1up))

**Sato, Aimaro** Court Councillor and Intendant to Prince Fushimi since '20; b. 1857 at Hirosaki; grad. at an American Univ. '81; Chief of the Telegraph Section Foreign Office, the same year; Sec. at Washington, '87; transferred to London, '91; Section Chief, '93; Sec. at Paris, '96 and next at Berlin; Min. to Mexico '00; recalled home in '02; on the suite of the Peace Plen. at Portsmouth '05; Minister to Holland '06; attended 2nd Peace Conference at the Hague '07 and Opium Conference at the Hague '11; transferred to Austro-Hungarian Embassy with additional post of Swiss Legation in '14; Ambassador to U.S.A. '16-18. Add. Azabu Toriizaka, Tokyo. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 886](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n886/mode/1up))

**Sato, Ko-ichi** D. Eng. (Architecture), Prof. at Waseda Univ. since '10; b. 1878 in Tochigi-ken; edu. at Imp. Tokyo Univ. in Architecture. class '10; travelled in Europe and America '10-11. 1st prize designs of the Tokyo City Hall, etc. Add. Koishikawa Sasugaya-cho, Tokyo. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 886](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n886/mode/1up))

**Sato, Naotake** Chief of the Imp. League of Nations Office in Geneva: b. 1882 in Okinawa; adopted by Mr. Aimaro Sato, diplomat, in 190[?]; grad. from Tokyo Higher Commercial School and passed Dip. Service Exam. '04; Attache '05; Consul-Gen. in Harbin till '21; Emb. Sec. at Paris '21-23; Minister to Poland '23-26. Add. Geneva. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 886](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n886/mode/1up))

**Sato, Sankichi** M.D., Crown Mem. House of Peers since '22; Emeritus Prof. of Imp. Tokyo Univ. '87-21; b. 1857 in Gifu-ken; learned mineralogy at the Tokyo Kaisei Gakko, but afterward took to Medicine which he finished in '82; sent to Germany for study '83-87; appoint. Prof. of his alma mater on return. Add. Sarugaku-cho, Tokyo. — p. 91 ([scan leaf 886](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n886/mode/1up))

**Sato, Sei-ichiro** M.D., Dir. of Surgical Dep't, Juntendo (private) Hospital; b. 1883 in Tokyo; grad. from the Fukuoka Med. Coll. affiliated to the Imp. Kyoto Univ., '90; was for a white assistant at the Fukuoka Coll.; after returning home he continued his study at the alma mater, and entered the Hospital '15. Add. Juntendo Hospital, Tokyo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n887/mode/1up))

**Sato, Shosuke** D. Agr., Ph. D. (Johns Hopkins'), Pres. of Hokkaido Univ. (Sapporo) since its creation; b. 1855 in Iwate-ken; grad. from the then existing Foreign Language School '76. and the Sapporo Agr. Coll. '80; further studied agri. economy at Johns Hopkins' and in Germany, '82-87; Professor in the Sapporo Coll. on returning home; its President, '94; American exchange Prof. '14; accorded ministerial rank, '24. Add. Sapporo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n887/mode/1up))

**Sato, Tatsujiro** M.D., Pres. of Junten-do (private) Hospital, 2nd Baron, being the adopted son of the late Baron Susumu Sato, founder of the Hospital, a noted surgeon of the day; b. 1868 in Wakasa; 2nd son of Sadasuke Kawai; Edu. at Coll. Med., Imp. Tokyo Univ., class '97; further studied in Germany, '97-00, and in Austria, '03-04. Succeeded to the adopted father's duty as Pres. of the Hospital on his death in '21; also acts as Dir. of the Tokyo Coll. of Medicine since '81. Add. Junten-do Hospital, Hongo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n887/mode/1up))

**Sato, Tsunamaru** M.D., Surg.-Gen. (ret.), Chief Physician in Ordinary to the Imperial Household; b. 1872 in Nagoya; edu. at Coll. Med., Imp. Tokyo Univ., class '96, and Post-Grad. course, '98; sent by Govt. to study at Berlin, Munchen, etc., '07-10; entered the Army in '97 as Surg. Sub-Lieut.; Surg. Maj., '04; Resident Surgeon in Germany, '07-10; Surg. Col., '12; Maj-Gen., '17; Surg.-Gen., '22; Pres. of Japan Red Cross Hospital '21-27; present post in Sept. '27. Add. Tokyo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n887/mode/1up))

**Sato, Ushijiro** D.C.L., Dean of College of Lit. Imp. Tohoku Univ. since '21; b. in 1877 in Yamagata-ken; studied politics at Imp. Kyoto Univ.; sent to Germany, France and England to further his studies '98-02; Prof. in the present Univ. on returning home. Add. Sendai. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n887/mode/1up))

**Sawada, Ken** political writer; b. in 1894 in Tottori-ken; at Imp. Tokyo Univ. in Law, class '18; commissioner of the Foreign Dept.; member of the Tokyo Municipal Research Commission. Works; Revolution of International Politics, Outline of the International League. Add Ushigome Nakamachi, Tokyo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n887/mode/1up))

**Sawada, Setsuzo** Emb. Counsellor in Washington; b. in Tottori-ken, 1884; passed Dip. Serv. Exam. while a student at Imp. Univ.; Sec. to Embassy at London '17-21; ordered to join the Crown Prince's suite when he visited Europe in '21; Chief of Secretariat at head office, '22-2[?]. Add. Jap. Emb., Washington. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n887/mode/1up))

**Segawa, Masao** M.D., a specialist in Children's Diseases. Dir. of the Segawa [illegible] Hospital etc.; b. 1884 in Sendai; Edu. at Coll. Med., Imp. Tokyo Univ., class '09; assist. at the alma mater, '09-13; proceeded to Berlin to further study his speciality, but had to return in '14 owing to the outbreak of the War. Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n887/mode/1up))

**Sejimo, Kiyomichi** D.C.L., at Columbia and Catholic University 1904. Barrister; b. 1865 in Sado; grad. from the now defunct English Law School '88; passed the Barristers' examination '88 and became lawyer the next year. Add. Kanda, Imagawakoji, Tokyo. — p. 92 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n887/mode/1up))

**Seki, Hajime** D.C.L., Mayor of Osaka since '24; b. in 1872; further studied in Tokyo; grad. from Higher Com. School in '93; further studied in Europe '98-01, communications being his speciality; was Prof. of the Tokyo Higher Com. School and Inspector of the Department of Education; Deputy-Mayor of Osaka, '14. Add. Osaka. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 888](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n888/mode/1up))

**Seki, Naohiko** lawyer, Crown Mem. House of Peers; b. '57 at Wakayama city; grad. law at Imp. Tokyo Univ. '83; once editor Wakayama city; of the Tokyo Nichi-Nichi; Mem. of Tokyo City Assembly; repeatedly elected M.P. since '90; Deputy Speaker of the Lower House in '29-3[?] sessions; is adviser to the Kakushin-to Party. Add. Kyobashi Minami-Kinrokucho, Tokyo. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 888](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n888/mode/1up))

**Sekiya, Teizaburo** Vice-Min. of Imp. Household; b. 1875 in Tochigi-ken; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '99; passed Higher Civil Service Exam. and entered the Home Office as Secretary; Secretary to Kwantung Govt. and local Govts. at home; Dir. of the Korean Education Dept., 1910; Gov. of Shizuoka prefecture 1919-21. Add. Imp. Household Dept. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 888](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n888/mode/1up))

**Semba, Taro** Lieut.-Gen. (ret.); b. 1855 in Aichi; Sub-Lieut. (infantry) in '78; entered the Staff College and further studied in Germany '90-93; took part in the China-Japan and Russia-Japan Wars; Lieut.-Gen. in '10, and a Section Chief for years in the General Staff Office; Div. Com. '10-15; M.P. in 1919. Add. Ichigaya, Tokyo. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 888](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n888/mode/1up))

**Senga, Tsurutaro** D.C.L., Dr. Jur. (Berlin), ex-Prof. International Law at Imp. Kyoto Univ.; b. in 186[?] in Okayama. Studied in Germany '84-89, first German literature and then law, both at Berlin University. He was sent by Government in 1900 to Europe and U.S.A. on tour of investigation. Works: Principles of International Law, Consular-Gerichtsbarkeit, etc. Add. Kyoto Imp. University. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 888](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n888/mode/1up))

**Sengoku, Mitsugu** D. Eng., ex-Min. of Railways 1924-26; b. in Kochi 1857; a pioneer grad. of Eng. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ. '79; long connected with the Govt. R'ly. Pres. of Kyushu R'ly Co. till its nationalization in '11; promoted Hydro-Elec. Co. '11; toured in Europe '11-12; Pres. of Inawashiro Hydro-Elec. Co. '12-14; Pres. of Imp. Govt. Rlys. '14-15. Add. Azabu Fujimi-cho, Tokyo. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 888](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n888/mode/1up))

**Seohimo, Kiyoshi** Mng.-Dir. of Mitsubishi Bank since '17; b. 1874, Nagano-ken; grad. from the Accountant's School formerly of Tokyo Higher Commercial School '93; entered the Mitsubishi Bank to become in time Sub-Manager and Manager of its branches at Osaka and Kobe; made a tour in Europe and America to inspect banking business '03-'10. Add. Takanawa-Kitamachi, Tokyo. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 888](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n888/mode/1up))

**Shiba, Chuzaburo** Baron, D. Eng., Prof. of Shipbuilding at Imp. Univ., Tokyo, and consulting engineer to the Kawasaki Dockyard; b. in Kaga in 1872; grad. from the University [illegible] '95, and afterward studied at the Central Technical College [illegible], London, and also at Humphrey and Tennant's Works and [illegible], at Berlin; undertook the designing of the Toyo [illegible] [illegible], sister steamers, Tenyo and Chiyo. Add. Hongo [illegible]ebono-cho, Tokyo. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 888](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n888/mode/1up))

**Shibusawa, Eiichi** Visc. (cr. Baron '00), founder of 1st Bank and Tokyo Savings Bank and for long President of Tokyo Chamber of Commerce, Chairman of Tokyo Bankers' Asso- ciation, etc. but recently retired from practical work retaining the post of Dir. of Municipal Orphanage of Tokyo, etc.; b. 1840 in Saitama-ken. Took service under the Tokugawa in its declining days and visited Europe '67-68 in company of a Tokugawa Prince; appointed to a high post in the Treasury on establishment of the Imperial Government, but left it, '73. From that time to his retirement he consistently kept aloof from Govt. service, though frequently offered the chair of Finance. Founded the 1st Bank (Dai-ichi Ginko), a pioneer of national bank in '73; chairman of the Tokyo Chamber of Commerce from its inauguration till spring of '15. Toured in Europe and America in '02; again in U.S.A. in '10, in '15 and in '21-22. Add. Oji, near Tokyo. — p. 93 ([scan leaf 888](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n888/mode/1up))

**Shibuya, Ariaki** Lieut.-Gen. (ret. '14) Court Councillor since '21; b. 1856 in Wakayama; Sub-Lieut. of Cavalry '79; entered Military Staff Coll. in '84; served for years as Aide-de-Camp to the Crown Prince, etc.; Lieut.-Gen. '08; Inspector of Commissariat Dept.; Dir., Imp. Mews, etc. Add. Azabu Gazenbo, Tokyo. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 889](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n889/mode/1up))

**Shida, Kotaro** D. L., ex-Prof. the Tokyo Commercial University; b. 1868 in Chiba Prefecture; grad. at Law Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ. '98; appointed Prof. at his alma mater in '06; engaged as Prof. and Adviser to Chinese Govt. to compile Com. and Civ. Law '08-12; long connected with Kyosai Life Ins. Co. Add. Koishi. Kobinatadai-machi, Tokyo. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 889](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n889/mode/1up))

**Shidachi, Tetsujiro** ex-Gov. of Ind. Bank of Japan; b. 1864 in Shimane-ken; grad. from Politics Imp. Tokyo Univ.; served the Bank of Japan till '89; entered Kyushu Railway '00; Sumitomo Bank '01-10; on the editorial staff of the Osaka Mainichi '11-12; Gov. of Ind. Bank '13-18. Add. Shimoshibuya, Tokyo. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 889](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n889/mode/1up))

**Shidehara, Kijuro** Baron (cr. '20), Min. of Foreign Affairs '24-27; b. in 1872 in Nara-ken; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '95 and served in Agr. and Com. Dept.; Eleve-Consul at Chemulpo '99; transferred to London, Antwerp, etc.; Sec. at Head Office '11; Embassy Counsellor at Washington in '12; Minister at The Hague '14-15; Vice-Min. of Foreign Affairs in '15; Ambassador to U.S.A. '19-22. Add. Hongo Kamifujimaye, Tokyo. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 889](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n889/mode/1up))

**Shiga, Kiyoshi** M.D., Dir., Govt. Hospital of Chosen and Pres. Seoul Medical Coll. since 1920; noted bacteriologist; b. in Sendai 1870; grad. from Medicine, Tokyo Imp. Univ. '96; entered the Infectious Diseases Investigation Laboratory; its Section Chief '99; studied further in Germany '01-04; sent to India by the Government '09 and to Italy and England '12; entered Dr. Kitazato's Laboratory in '14, later combining a chair of bacteriology at the Medical Coll., Keio Univ. Add. Seoul. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 889](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n889/mode/1up))

**Shijo, Takahide** (Baron) Perm. Vice-Min. of Com. and Ind. Dept. since '25; b. of an ancient family in 1876; grad. in Politics, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '04; entered Dep't of Com. and Agr. in which he successively held post of Secretary, Sectional Chief and finally Dir. of Industrial Bureau. Add. Dept. of Com. & Ind. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 889](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n889/mode/1up))

**Shimazaki, Haruki** (nom de plume Toson), a novelist and man of letters; b. 1872 in Nagano-ken; edu. at Meiji Gakuin, Methodist school. Works; Toson's Collected Poems, "Hakai", "Iye" and other novels; complete works issued in '22. Add. Azabu Iigura, Tokyo. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 889](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n889/mode/1up))

**Shimazono, Junjiro** M.D., Prof. of Internal Med., Imp. Kyoto Univ., Pres. of Internal Med. Society of Japan, etc.; b. 1879 in Wakayama-ken. Edu. at Coll. Med., Imp. Tokyo Univ., class '04; one-year volunteer in the Army in '04 and as Surg.-Lieutenant went through active service in the Russo-Japanese War; placed on the reserve list in '07; assist. at the alma mater, '07-11. Proceeded to Germany to study his speciality, '11-13; Prof. at Okayama Medical Coll., '13; Prof. at Kyoto Imp. Univ. since '16. Add. Imp. Kyoto Univ. — p. 94 ([scan leaf 889](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n889/mode/1up))

**Shimazu, Tadashige** Prince, formerly of the fief of Satsuma, and 30th head of the line which was founded in 12th century; his predecessor Seihin and uncle Hisamitsu did much for rehabilitating the Imperial power. The present head was b. 188[?] and is Commander of Navy. His sister is Princess Kuni. Visited Europe in '21 with his wife. Add. Kojimachi Nagatacho, Tokyo. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 890](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n890/mode/1up))

**Shimizu, Cho** D.C.L., Judge of Adm. Litigation Court; b. 186[?] in Kanagawa; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '94; Secretary of the Home Office and Prof. at the Peers' School and studied in Germany and Austria '98-99. Add. Yotsuya Aizumicho, Tokyo. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 890](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n890/mode/1up))

**Shimizu, Kamezo** master glyptic art and Prof. at Govt. Fine Art School in Tokyo, in charge of the Metal Dept., from which he was graduated in '96; teacher till '15 at a Technical School in Kagawa; studied old arts and Buddhist philosophy at Nara Horyuji for three years; appointed to the present chair in '19; Works; undertook the casting of metal decorative figures presented by the City of Tokyo to the Emperor and Empress at their silver wedding in '25. Add. Fine Art School, Uyeno, Tokyo. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 890](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n890/mode/1up))

**Shimizu, Teikichi** Capt. Infantry (retired), Dir., Ishikawajima Dockyard, Tokyo; b. 186[?] in Kyoto; adopted by Mannosuke Shimizu; grad. from Eng., Imp. Tokyo Univ., 1900 and took up his adopted father's business, Shimizu-gumi, Public Work Contractor; toured abroad, 1901. Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 890](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n890/mode/1up))

**Shimoda, Utako** Mad., Dir. Jissen Girls School and Pres. of the Women's Patriotic Association since '20; a Court maid of honour when about 16 years old, and again after the death of her husband. An instructor of the Peeress' School; resigned in Nov. '07 and has since devoted herself chiefly to a private female school she founded about '99; visited Europe '98-9[?]. Add. Aoyama Kitamachi, Tokyo. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 890](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n890/mode/1up))

**Shimojo, Yasumaro** Dir. of the Statistical Bureau since '24; b. 1885 in Tokyo; grad. from Imp. Tokyo Univ., Law, class '10. Served as jun. sec. of the provincial and central government such as chief of the Archive Sec., Cabinet; then Dir. of the Pension Bureau, and lastly to the above post. Add. Statistical Bureau. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 890](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n890/mode/1up))

**Shimomura, Hiroshi** Dir. of the Tokyo Asahi since '21; b. 1875; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '98, and was long connected with postal service as Dir. Postal Money Order Board; Dir. of Civil Adm., Formosa '15-21. Add. Tokyo Asahi. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 890](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n890/mode/1up))

**Shimura, Gentaro** Mem. House of Peers, Pres. of Central Bank for Coop. Societies founded '2[?], etc.; b. in 186[?] in Yamanashi; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '89 and entered Agr. and Com. Dept; Counsellor '93; Section Chief in '97; Vice-Gov. of the Hypothec Bank '02-12; travelled through Europe and America for inspection of business '99-01; Gov. of Japan Hypothec Bank '12-21. Add. Koishikawa Kanatomicho, Tokyo. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 890](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n890/mode/1up))

**Shiohara, Matasaku** Dir. of Sankyo Kabushiki-Kaisha; b. in Yokohama in 1880, and studied at Yokohama Commercial School and Yokohama English and Japanese Language School; started drug business in '99; went to America to study drug business. Add. Azabu Iigura-kata-machi, Tokyo. — p. 95 ([scan leaf 890](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n890/mode/1up))

**Shiota, Hirohige** M.D., Prof. of Surgery at Coll. Med., Imp. Tokyo Univ. and Chief Surgeon of the Univ. Hospital; b. 187[?] in Kyoto-fu; grad. from above Univ., class '99; assist. at the alma mater till '07; studied his speciality at Vienna, '07-09; has been connected with his Univ. till present. Elected Pres. of the Japan Surgical Society, '19. Add. Hongo Yumi-cho, Tokyo. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 891](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n891/mode/1up))

**Shiozawa, Shotei** Ph. D. (Michigan), D.L. (Japan), Prof. at Waseda Univ.; b. in 1874 at Mito; grad. from Waseda in '95, from Wisconsin '00, and further studied at Berlin and Halle '01-02, his speciality being political economy; Pres. of the Univ. '21-23. Add. Waseda Univ. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 891](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n891/mode/1up))

**Shiraishi, Motojiro** Dir. of the Tokyo Kisen Kaisha, Hinode Life Ins. Co. etc., b. 186[?] in Niigata-ken; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. and entered Toyo Kisen Kaisha; founded Nippon Steel Tube Mfg. Co. over which he presides. Add. Mita-Kouncho, Tokyo. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 891](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n891/mode/1up))

**Shiraiwa, Ryuhei** Mng. Dir. Toa Industrial Co., and Dir. of Nisshin (Japan-China) S.S. Co.; b. 1870 in Okayama-ken; studied at Nisshin Boeki Kenkyujo (Japan-China Trading Institute), Shanghai; attached to the Imperial Headquarters as Military Interpreter in the '94-95 War. Add. Aoyama Minami-cho, Tokyo. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 891](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n891/mode/1up))

**Shirakawa, Yoshinori** Gen., Minister of War since Apr. '27; b. 1870 in Ehime-ken; Sub.-Lt. Infantry '19; Capt. '98; Col. '09; Maj.-Gen. '15; took part in the Russo-Japanese War; Lt.-Gen. and Dir. of the Military Staff Coll. '18; dispatched to Siberia as Com. of the 11th Div. '21-22; Vice-Min. of War '22-24; Commander of Kwantung Army '25-27. Add. War Office, Tokyo. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 891](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n891/mode/1up))

**Shirani, Takeshi** Pres. of Nippon Yusen Kaisha since '24; b. 186[?] in Fukuoka ken; grad. at Politics, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '90; entered Home Office and rose to Dir. of Temple and Shrine Affairs Bureau '02; also served at Hokkaido Office, and at the Edu. Dept.; Gov. of Tochigi-ken, '03; Dir. Ordinary Edu. Bureau, '06-08; Dir. of Civil Gov. Kwantung, '08-16; Dir. of Colonial Bureau, '18; Pres., Imp. Iron Foundry, '18-24. Add. Waseda Tsurumaki-cho, Tokyo. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 891](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n891/mode/1up))

**Shiratori, Kurakichi** historian, Litt. D., ex-tutor to the Crown Prince, Emeritus Prof. at Peers' School and Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. 1865 in Chiba; grad. in '99 from Lit. Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ., further studied in Germany and Hungary '00-05; is an authority on Mongolian dynasties and allied subjects. Add. Shimo-Ochiai, suburb of Tokyo. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 891](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n891/mode/1up))

**Siratori, Toshio** Emb. Secretary in Berlin; b. in Chiba-ken, 1887; passed Dip. and Con. Serv. Exam. '13; grad. from Economics, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '19; Eleve Consul first at Mukden and then Hongkong; attache at Washington '16; Sec. '18; Sec. at the For. Office '22-2[?]. Add. Berlin. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 891](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n891/mode/1up))

**Sho, Seijiro** Mng. of Mitsubishi Firm; b. 1862 in Nagasaki; grad. Tokyo Univ. '84; studied in U.S.A.; and has since been connected with the Firm. Add. Koishikawa Kobinata Suido-cho, Tokyo. — p. 96 ([scan leaf 891](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n891/mode/1up))

**Shoda, Kazue** former Min. of Finance ('16-18), Crown Mem. House of Peers (nom. '14); b. 1869 in Ehime-ken; graduated from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '95; passed the higher civil service examination in the following year; inspector of taxation affairs '07; Dir. of Finance Bureau, Treasury in '07; Vice-Minister in '11-14, having been in the meanwhile Chief of Hakodate Customs House, Sec. at the Head Office, etc.; Gov. of Chosen Bank in '15; Vice-Minister of Finance '16, soon becoming full Minister. Add. Nakashibuya, Tokyo. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 892](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n892/mode/1up))

### Soe—Soye

**Soga, Sukenori** Lieut.-Gen. (retired), Viscount (cr. '84), Privy Councillor, ex-President of nationalized Nippon Railway Co., and Mem. House of Peers '91-1[?]; b. 184? in Saga-ken; early entered the Imp. Army; took distinguished part as Commander of Brigade on the occasion of Civil War of '77; subsequently a Divisional Commander at various places; held for a while the post of Vice-Chief of the General Staff; retired from active service about '87; was once chief military tutor and Aide-de-Camp to the Crown Prince. Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 892](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n892/mode/1up))

**Soma, Hanji** M.A., Pres. of Meiji Sugar Co., Formosa; b. 1869 at Inuyama; non-commissioned officer '85-90; studied at the Tokyo Higher Technical School; then Berlin and Michigan, devoting himself to chemical researches about sugar; taught at his alma mater '03-04; chemist to Formosan Government-Gen.; Dir. of the present Co. established '06. Add. Shiba Park, Tokyo. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 892](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n892/mode/1up))

**Soyeda, Juichi** D.L., Mem. House of Peers; b. 186[?] in Fukuoka-ken; grad. from Pol. Economy of the Imp. Tokyo Univ., '84; further studied at Cambridge and Heidelberg; a Councillor of the Treasury on his return home, '87; Vice-Minister, '98; Pres. of the Bank of Formosa, '99 when the bank was inaugurated; chairman of the Organization Com. of the Japan Industrial Bank and its President on its being formally started '02-12; visited U.S.A. in conection with anti-Japanese agitation in '13; President of Imp. Gov't Railways in '15-16. Add. Koj. Fujimicho, Tokyo. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 892](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n892/mode/1up))

**Soyejima, Michimasa** 2nd Count (1st Count Taneomi, d. '05, distinguished himself in the Restoration); Mem. of House of Peers; b. in 1871; edu. at the Peers' School and then at Leys School, Cambridge, England, and Cambridge University where he studied history under Prof. Seeley, class '95; lecturer at Chicago Univ. in '25. Add. Azabu Kogai-cho, Tokyo. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 892](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n892/mode/1up))

**Suehiro, Iwataro** D.L., Prof. at Imp. Tokyo Univ., Law; b. 1888 in Yamaguchi-ken; edu. at Imp. Tokyo Univ. and also at Western Univ. for about three years. Works; Law of Obligations, Law of Things, etc. He took active part in the relief work in connection with the earthquake disaster of 1923. Add. Koishikawa Takehaya-cho, Tokyo. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 892](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n892/mode/1up))

**Suehiro, Shigewo** D.L., Prof. at Kyoto Imp. Univ; b. 1874 in Uwajima; edu. at Tokyo Imp. Univ. in Law, class '99; studied Diplomacy in Europe '01-05. Appointed Lecturer at Imp. Kyoto Univ. '01; studied on official order Political History in Germany and England, etc., '01-05; Prof. at Kyoto, '06; visit to U.S.A. and Mexico to investigate immigration conditions in '14; Dean of the Law Coll. '19-22. Works; Japanese in U.S.A., etc. Add. Kyoto Imp. University. — p. 97 ([scan leaf 892](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n892/mode/1up))

**Sugano, Shoichi** Gen. Supreme War Councillor; b. in Yamaguchi-ken in 1870; studied at the Staff Coll.; Sub.-Lt. '92; Lt.-Gen. '19; stayed in Belgium and England as Attache; Dir. of the Mil. Affairs Bureau, War Office and then Div. Com., etc.; Vice-Min. of War, '21-22; Com. of Taiwan Army, '24-27. Was on the staff of Gen. Nogi's Army the 1904 war. Add. War Office, Tokyo. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 893](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n893/mode/1up))

**Sugawara, Kenji** D.L., Lawyer and member of the Osaka Bar.; b. 1884 in Hyogo-ken; grad. from German Law, Imp. Kyoto Univ., class '09; Probationery Judge the same year; passed Judicial Exam. with highest honor in '02; Judge of the Tokyo District Court and at the same time taught at several private law institutions; assist.-prof. at Kyoto, '05; sent abroad for further study, '15-18; Prof. at the alma mater with which he was connected till '24. Add. Okazaki Irie-cho, Kyoto. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 893](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n893/mode/1up))

**Sugawara, Michitaka** Mem. House of Peers since '16 and for several years Mng.-Dir. of the Japan-American Trust Co.; b. 1869 in Miyagi-ken; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '96; entered the service of the Treasury; held post of Dir. of Revenue, Customs Office, at Hakodate, Kobe, etc.; Councillor in '04, then Dir. Tax Bureau; Vice-Min. Finance '15-16. Add. Koishikawa Kagocho, Tokyo. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 893](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n893/mode/1up))

**Sugimori, Kojiro** Prof. at Waseda; b. 1881 in Shizuoka-ken; edu. at Waseda, in Literature, class '06; further studied philosophy as student sent by Government in England and Germany '13-19. Works: The Principle of the Moral Empire (in English), Resurrection of Men, Foundation of New Society, etc. Add. Waseda Univ. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 893](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n893/mode/1up))

**Sugimoto, Etsuko** Mrs., Prof. of History of Japanese civilisation at Columbia Univ. (U.S.A.); grad. from Aoyama Gakuin; went to Cincinnati, Ohio, in '97 with her husband, and studied by herself while dealing in grocery; widowed in '10. Become Prof. at Columbia Univ. '20; Add. Columbia Univ., New York. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 893](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n893/mode/1up))

**Sugimura, Kotaro** author and journalist on the staff of the Tokyo Asahi; b. 1872 in Wakayama; first was school teacher and then translator at U.S. Embassy, before he entered Asahi '04; travelled several times in the West. Has written a number of books. Add. Abiko, Chiba-ken, near Tokyo. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 893](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n893/mode/1up))

**Sugimura, Yotaro** Deputy Chief of the League of Nations Office in Geneva, Minister unattached; b. 1884 in Tokyo. Edu. at Imp. Tokyo Univ. in Politics, class '08 and passed the Dip. and Con. Exam. the same year; Eleve-Con. at Lyons '08; Attache in France, '12; 3rd Emb. Sec., '14; 3rd Leg. Sec. in China; 2nd Sec, '17; recalled in '19 and appointed Mng. of the Temporary Treaty Revision Committee; Sec. and Coun. and Sectional Chief the same year; Assis. to the Imp. Envoy to the Washington Conference '21 and to the Imp.-Delegate to the Wartime Law Revision Commission at The Hague, '22; 1st Emb. Sec. in Paris; Jr. Mem. in the 4th Congress of the League of Nations at Geneva, and has also attended similar such international conferences held at Geneva; Deputy-Chief of the Imp. League of Nations Office at Paris and Embassy Counsellor there '23-27. Add. Geneva, Switzerland. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 893](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n893/mode/1up))

**Sugino, Kisei** Mng. Dir. of Yamaichi & Co.; b. in 1870. In Hirosaki, Aomori-ken; grad. '89 from the Banking Training Course attached to Treasury and entered the Bank of Japan; Mng.-Dir. of Nagoya Ginko Ltd. 1901; entered Koike & Co., Tokyo, as its manager '10; and on its voluntary winding up in '17 created the present concern. Office, Kabuto-cho, Tokyo. — p. 98 ([scan leaf 893](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n893/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**Sugita, Teiichi** Mem. of House of Peers; b. 1851 in Fukui-ken; is one of the dwindling band of old Liberals who struggled long for the cause of freedom by sacrificing personal interest and comfort; Gov. of Hokkaido in the Okuma-Itagaki Cabinet, '98; Vice-Pres. of House of Rep. '03; its Pres. '05-08; nom. Crown Mem. of House of Peers in '12. Add. Shimoshibuya, Tokyo. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 894](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n894/mode/1up))

**Suenobu, Dosei** Pres. Tokyo Marine Ins., Meiji Life Ins. Cos., and of several other important concerns; b. 1855 in Kochi-ken and was among the first batch of graduates of the Imp. Univ., Tokyo; has since devoted himself to business. Add. Azabu Torizaka, Tokyo. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 894](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n894/mode/1up))

**Suzuki, Bunji** Labor leader and Pres. of the General Federation of Labor in Japan; b. 1885 in Miyagi-ken; grad. from Politics, Imp. Tokyo Univ., class '09. Before he organized in '12 the Yu-ai Kai, the first Friendly Society after Western model, he served at a printing shop, a newspaper office and was also manager of the Japan Unitarian Association. Represented Labor at Geneva, 1924 Conference. Add. Totsuka Suwa, near Tokyo. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 894](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n894/mode/1up))

**Suzuki, Kantaro** Adm. (ret.); b. 1865 in Chiba Prefecture; grad. from Naval Academy in '86; Lieut.-Com. '98; Commander '02; Capt. '03; once Dir. of Torpedo School; Vice-Minister of Navy in '14; Dir. of the Naval Staff Coll. '19; Com. of the 2nd Squadron '21; Com.-in-Chief of Sasebo Adm. '22; full Adm. and Com.-in-Chief of 1st Fleet, '24; Mem. of Supreme War Council in '25. Add. Sugamo, Tokyo. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 894](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n894/mode/1up))

**Suzuki, Fujiya** M.P., Lawyer; b. 1882 in Oita-ken; grad. Law Imp. Tokyo Univ.; toured abroad 1906-08; Parl. Counsellor, Home Office '24-27. Add. Ginza 1 chome, Tokyo. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 894](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n894/mode/1up))

**Suzuki Kisaburo** D.C.L., Minister of Interior; b. in Kawasaki '67; grad. from Law Coll. of the Imp. Tokyo Univ., in '91, and sat on the bench since '95; meanwhile occupying the posts of Chief Judge at Tokyo District Court, Dir. Legal Affairs Bur., etc.; was dispatched abroad in '08 on a tour of inspection; Vice-Min. Dept. of Justice, '14; Procurator-Gen. '21-24; Min. of Justice '24. Add. Ushigome Yamabushicho, Tokyo. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 894](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n894/mode/1up))

**Suzuki, Shimakichi** Gov. of the Bank of Korea since '25; b. 1868; grad. from Keio '89; entered the Yokohama Specie Bank, '92; Sub. Manager of its New York Branch '95; Manager of the Tientsin, Peking and Shanghai Branches in succession; resigned the post of Vice-Pres. in '22. Add. Bank of Korea, Tokyo. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 894](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n894/mode/1up))

**Suzuki, Umetaro** Dr. Agr., Prof. & Dean of Agr. Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ., and discoverer of vitamin; b. 1875 in Shizuoka-ken; grad. from Agr. Chemistry above Coll. in '97 and was app. four years later its assist. Prof.; scientific tour in Germany, France and Switzerland '03; Prof. at the Morioka Higher Agr. and Forestry School and later transferred to his alma mater; attended the 8th International Applied Chemistry Conference held in New York. Add. Shibuya-machi, Tokyo. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 894](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n894/mode/1up))

**Tachibana, Koichiro** Gen. (ret.), Mayor of Fukuoka; b. 1861 at Fukuoka; grad. Cadets School '83; Cap. '94; Major '98; Colonel and Adjutant to the War Minister '05; Maj.-Gen. '09; Chief Staff of the Korean Garrison and Com. of Gendarmerie in Korea in '14; Div. Com. in '16; full Gen. and Com. of the Kwantung Garrison. '19-21; Com. of the Siberian Expedition, '21-22; retired '23. Add. Fukuoka. — p. 99 ([scan leaf 894](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n894/mode/1up))

**Tadokoro, Yoshiharu** Crown Mem. House of Peers.; Pres. Kyodo Fire Ins. Co.; b. 1871 in Kochi; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '95; passed higher civil service examination the following year; Counsellor at various local offices and at Edu. Dep't; was in Europe '03-05 to investigate educational administration; Commissioner of Anglo-Japan Exhibition '10; Dir. General Education Bureau '11-16; Vice-Minister of Edu., '16-18. Add. Koj. Dote-sambancho, Tokyo. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 895](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n895/mode/1up))

**Tagawa, Daikichiro** M.P., Pres. Meiji Gakuin; b. in 1869 in Nagasaki-ken; grad. from the Waseda Semmon Gakko (now Univ.) in '90; took part in Sino-Japanese and Russo-Japanese Wars as interpreter; is a prominent Christian thinker; was imprisoned in '18 on charge of lese majeste for a magazine article; Parl. Sec'y of Justice Dept., in Okuma Cabinet and ex-Deputy-Mayor of Tokyo. Add. Kobinatadaimachi, Tokyo. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 895](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n895/mode/1up))

**Takagi, Kikan** Baron (2nd), M.D., Mem. House of Peers, Prof. Tokyo Jikei-in Medical Coll.; b. 1874, son of the late Surgeon-Gen. Takagi Kenkan; studied medicine in England. Add. Azabu Higashi-Toriizaka, Tokyo. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 895](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n895/mode/1up))

**Takagi, Masutaro** M.P. and barrister; b. in 1869 in Tokyo; grad. from the Tokyo Hogaku-in in '91; passed the Judge and Procurators' Examination and Barristers' in '95, and has since been practising in Tokyo. Add. Nihombashi Honshiro[illegible]echo, Tokyo. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 895](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n895/mode/1up))

**Takagi, Rokuro** Japanese Representative of Han-Yeh-Ping Iron and Coal Mining Co.; b. in Miyagi-ken 1880; had only middle school education before entering Mitsui Bussan Kaisha in 1900; was despatched to China by the Co. as a commercial student; took part in Boxers' Trouble as Military Interpreter; later he served at Mitsui's Branches at Hankow and Shanghai. Left the Co. in '11 on the establishment of the I. & C. Co. Add. Ushigome Nijukkicho, Tokyo. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 895](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n895/mode/1up))

**Takahama, Kiyoshi** (pen name Kyoshi), Haiku poet; b. 1874 in Iyo; left the Third High School unfinished; has since been taking prominent part in the haiku poetry and as a man of letters. Add. Kamakura Haranodai. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 895](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n895/mode/1up))

**Takahashi, Kamekichi** a financial writer; b. at Tokuyama in 1892 and was a shop boy till about '20; edu. at Waseda Univ., in Commerce, class '16; entered the service of Kuhara Mining Co.; was on the staff of the Oriental Economist (Tokyo) '18-26. Works: Practical Knowledge of Economy. Add. Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 895](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n895/mode/1up))

**Takahashi, Kazutomo** Prof. Keio Univ.; b. 1862 in Kawagoye; entered Law Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ., but left in '86; grad. from Michigan State Univ.; attended to business in the States and Canada before he returned home '97 to join the Japan Times (Tokyo) of which he was chief editor for many years; then Translator to the Foreign Office in 1923. Add. Tsunohazu, Tokyo. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 895](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n895/mode/1up))

**Takahashi, Koi** M.P., Dir. of the Secretariat Bureau in the Hara Ministry, Dir. of Horai Life Ins., etc.; b. 1867 in Niigata-ken; studied at Keio '93; dispatched abroad by the Dept. of Agriculture and Commerce; journalist for a time; twice Secretary to Mr. Hara during his accession to Home Office. Add. 19 Shiba Nishikubo-Akefunecho, Tokyo. — p. 100 ([scan leaf 895](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n895/mode/1up))

**Takahashi, Korekiyo** M.P., ex-Visc.; b. 1845 in Tokyo; sent to America for study '67; was for several months treated as a slave through the treachery of his knavish American "guardian"; returned home the following year; an official of the Dep't. Agr. and Com., '81, ultimately rising to be Dir. Patent Bureau. Was involved in the Peruvian silver mine fraud concocted by a German imposter and resigned the Bureau in '87; entered the Bank of Japan, and was made a Director; Vice-Pres. of the Yokohama Specie Bank; '97; elected Vice-Gov., Bank of Japan, then made Pres. of the Specie Bank '06, retaining the former post. Financial agent in England and America, and raised loans in '05 and '06; Gov. of the Bank of Japan in '11; Minister of Finance '13-14, and again in '18; promoted to Visc. in '20 for his service in connection with the European War; Premier '21-22; resigned the Peerage in favor of his son and seat in the Upper House to contest the seat for Morioka in '24; resigned Leadership of Seiyukai, '25; Minister of Finance in the Tanaka Cabinet, Apr.-May '27. Add. Akasaka Omote-cho, Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 896](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n896/mode/1up))

**Takahashi, Seigo** Ph. D. (Columbia) and Prof. at Waseda which he graduated in Politics, class '11; b. 1891 in Miyagi-ken. Works: Political Idea in Europe, Development of Social Institutions in Europe, Construction of Cities. Add. Waseda Univ. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 896](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n896/mode/1up))

**Takahashi, Sei-ichiro** Prof. at Keio Univ. his alma mater; further studied in Europe specializing History of Economics. Works: Investigations of History of Economics, History of European Economics. Add. Keio Univ. Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 896](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n896/mode/1up))

**Takahata, Motoyuki** socialist; b. 1887 in Maebashi and after finishing a middle school studied two years at Divinity Dept., Doshisha; joined the "Scriveners" Club founded by K. Sakai, socialist. Works: Translation of Complete Works of Marx, Exposition of Capitalism, Socialism and Evolution, Socialism and Sociology, etc. Add. Hongo Sendagi, Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 896](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n896/mode/1up))

**Takakusu, Junjiro** M.A. (Oxf.) Litt. D., Prof. at Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. in Bingo 1865; edu. at Oxford '88 and studied Sanskrit; further in Germany and returned home '97; Prof. at Imp. Tokyo Univ.; Personal Secretary to the Minister of Communications (Viscount Suyematsu); President of Foreign Language School, '00-08; stayed in London with the Viscount during the Russo-Japanese War; sent abroad '10-12. Works; translated several Sanskrit and Pali works. Add. Koishikawa Sekiguchi Daimachi, Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 896](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n896/mode/1up))

**Takamatsu, Toyokichi** D. Eng., Emeritus Prof. of Imp. Univ. Tokyo; ex-Dir. of State Industrial Laboratory, etc.; b. 1855 in Tokyo, grad. at Chemistry at Imp. Tokyo Univ., in '78; further studied at Owens Coll., Manchester, and at Berlin Univ., returning home in '82; Prof. at his alma mater and other institutions before he retired from official career in '05; the Pres. of Tokyo Gas Co. '05-15. Add. Sugamo, Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 896](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n896/mode/1up))

**Takamura, Ko-un** master-artist in wood carving, Prof. in Gov. Fine Art Academy; b. 1852 in Tokyo; studied under Takamura To-un, a celebrated Buddhist image carver in Tokyo who died '79; undertook wood model for the bronze image of Nanko erected in front of the Imp. Palace and of the Senior Saigo at Ueno Park. Won a gold medal at the Paris Fair '00; frequently honored to carve in presence of their Majesties. Art Commissioner to Imp. Household. Add. Komagome Hayashi-cho, Tokyo. — p. 101 ([scan leaf 896](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n896/mode/1up))

**Takano, Iwasaburo** D.C.L., Dir. of Ohara Social Institute, Osaka, statist and labor expert; b. 1871 in Tokyo; studied Law at the Imp. Tokyo Univ.; assis.-Prof. at the alma mater '99; sent to France, Germany and U.S.A. for studying statistics; full Prof. and doctorate in 1903; again toured abroad '09; elected Labor Delegate to Washington in '18 but resigned; left Gov't service in '19. His wife is a German by birth. Add. Osaka. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 897](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n897/mode/1up))

**Takaoka, Kumazo** D.L., and D. Agr., Dean at Hokkaido Imp. Univ., (Sapporo); b. 1875 in Shimane-ken; grad. from Sapporo Agr. Coll., class '95; assist.-Prof. in '97; studied in Germany '00-04 Agr. Economics. Works; Agriculture in Hokkaido; Agricultural Politics; Agricultural Education in the U.S.A., etc. Add. Sapporo. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 897](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n897/mode/1up))

**Takarabe, Hyo** Adm., Supreme War Councillor; b. 1867 in Hyuga; graduated from Naval Cadet School '90; on staff at the headquarters in '94-95 and '04-05 wars; Capt. '95; Vice-Adm. '1[?] and Vice-Min. of Navy, '13-14; put on waiting list for a while in connection with the Naval Scandal in '14; Com. of 3rd Squadron in '17; Com. of Port Arthur Naval Station '15-0; Com. Saseho Naval Station. '18-22; then similar post at Yokosuka; Minister of Navy, '23-27. Add. Shiba Shirokane Sanko-cho, Tokyo. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 897](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n897/mode/1up))

**Takashima, Kikujiro** Dir. of Oji Paper Mill; b. 1875 in Fukuoka-ken; grad. from Tokyo Higher Commercial School '00; entered the Osaka Shosen Kaisha; removed to Mitsui Bussan Kaisha '03; to the present Co. '12. Add. Takinogawa near Tokyo. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 897](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n897/mode/1up))

**Takata, Kamakichi** Formerly of Takata Firm, the celebrated as pioneer exporters & importers (which was declared insolvent in '25); b. in Yokohama in 1876, 3rd son of the late Tanaka Heihachi; grad. from Berlin Univ. in 1901; served in the Shibaura Iron Works '02-04; in the Tokyo Electric Works for years before he became connected with the Takata Firm. Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 897](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n897/mode/1up))

**Takata, Koan** M.D., Proprietor of Takata Hospital, Tokyo, and Nan-ko-in Hospital, Chigasaki; b. 1861 in Kyoto; grad. from Med. Coll., Imp. Tokyo Univ. '90 and subsequently commenced practice, lung troubles being his speciality. Add. Kojimachi Sambancho, Tokyo. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 897](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n897/mode/1up))

**Takata, Sanae** D.C.L., Crown Mem. House of Peers, (nom. '15), President of Waseda Univ.; b. 1860 at Tokyo; grad. at Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '82; has since been connected with Waseda Univ., which was started the same year by Marquis Okuma; was once one of the leading members of the Progressionists, but for years past kept aloof from active politics, devoting himself to affairs of the Univ.; was Director of the Commercial Bureau, Foreign Office, '96-97 and Senior Counsellor of Education, '98; was repeatedly returned to the House from Saitama-ken; toured in Europe and America '14; Minister of Education in the Okuma Cabinet in '15-16. Add. Komagome, Tokyo. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 897](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n897/mode/1up))

**Takayama, Choko** M.P., ex-Pres. of Teikoku Commercial Bank; b. 1867 in Ehime-ken; grad. from Keio; entered Mitsui Bank '93. Add. Shibuya-machi, Tokyo. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 897](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n897/mode/1up))

**Takebe, Kin-ichi** Dir. Common Edu. Bureau; b. 1881; grad. at Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '08; passed Higher Civil Service Exam; Secretary and Section Chief of the Dep't till '22; Dir. Technical Edu. Bureau, '22-27. Add. Nishi-katamachi, Tokyo. — p. 102 ([scan leaf 897](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n897/mode/1up))

**Takechi, Nobmichi** Dir. Tokyo Trust Co., Aud. Nippon Conscription Ins., etc.; b. 1870; studied at Keio and also at a Univ. in Hawaii; was once in diplomatic service. Add. Azabu Ichibei-cho, Tokyo. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 898](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n898/mode/1up))

**Takeda, Hideo** Vice-Adm. of Eng. (ret.), chairman of Dirs., Mitsubishi Shipbuilding Co.; b. 1864 in Kochi-ken; grad. from the Naval Eng. School, '83; studied in France, '91-92; Commander, '97; Vice-Adm. in '13; Dir. of his alma mater; entered the present Co. in '14. Took part in 1894-5 and '04-5 Wars. Add. Koishikawa Haramachi, Tokyo. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 898](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n898/mode/1up))

**Takekoshi, Yosaburo** Crown Mem. House of Peers since '22, author and journalist; b. 1865 in Tokyo; studied at the Dojinsha and Keio-gijuku; on the staff of the Jiji; Pers. Sec. of Marquis Saionji '98; app. in '21 Chief Compiler of Dept. Imp. Household; was M.P. before he entered the Dept. Works; "History of 2,[?]00 Years of Japan" and other works. Add. Higashi-Okubo, near Tokyo. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 898](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n898/mode/1up))

**Takeshita, Isamu** Adm., Mem. Supreme Military Council since '25; b. 1869 in Kagoshima-ken; Sub-Lieut. '90; Commander '07; Capt. '11; Rear-Adm. '13; Vice-Adm. '17; in the meantime Naval attache to Washington. Represented Japan's Navy in the Paris Peace Conference '18; tutor to the Crown Prince and accompanied H.H. to Europe in '21; Vice-Chief the Naval Gen. Staff, '21-22; full Admiral and Com. of First Fleet, '23; Com.-in-Chief of Kure Naval Station '24-25. Add. Navy Dept. Tokyo. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 898](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n898/mode/1up))

**Takatomi, Tokitoshi** Mem. House of Peers since '24; b. 1855, in Saga-ken; from obscure politician of local reputation he suddenly came to the front as soon as he began to sit in the Lower House where he was returned almost without a break from the first session; Chief Secretary of the Cabinet in Okuma-Itagaki Ministry; Minister of Communications in 2nd Okuma Ministry; occupied portfolio of Finance '15-16; retired from active politics in '24 but still acts as Adviser to the Minsei-to Party. Add. Ushigome Kaga-cho, Tokyo. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 898](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n898/mode/1up))

**Takeuchi, Teizaburo** Gen.-Mng. of the Yasuda Bank; b. 1876 in Hokkaido; adopted by the family; was long connected with the Higo Bank in which he rose to section chief and then Mng. Dir. Add. Yasuda Bank, Tokyo. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 898](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n898/mode/1up))

**Takeuchi, Tsunekichi** (nom de plume Seiho), Painter-in-Ordinary to the Court, Prof. at Kyoto Fine Art Academy, Mem. of the Salon, Paris, in '22; is one of the living master painters of the Japanese school; b. 1864 at Kyoto and studied under the late Bairei; visited Europe '00. Add. Milke-dori, Yanagikoji, Kyoto. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 898](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n898/mode/1up))

**Taki, Sei-ichi** Litt. D., Professor of Oriental Art, Imp. University, Tokyo; b. in 187[?], a son of Taki Katei, a master-painter in his day; grad. from Imp. Univ. Tokyo in '96, and further studied art criticism in England and France. He has been a regular correspondent to the London Studio and edits an art journal Kokka. Add. Tsukiji, Tokyo. — p. 103 ([scan leaf 898](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n898/mode/1up))
