**Yoshiuye, Sho-ichiro, P.M** leader of the Seiyu-kai; b. 1865 in Chiba; was for a long time in Hokkaido as editor of a local paper and for which he first entered House of Representatives but now for his native province; visited Chile years ago on Gov. commission to investigate condition of immigrants; Parl. Vice.-Min. of Com. & Ind. '27. Add. Akasaka Daimachi, Tokyo. — p. 124 ([scan leaf 919](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n919/mode/1up))

**Yoshizawa, Kenkichi** Minister to China since '23; b. 1874 in Niigata-ken; grad. from Literature (English Lit.), Imp. Tokyo Univ. '99; passed Dip. Service Exam. the same year; attache at Seoul '99; Eleve-Consul at Amoy, Shanghai, London, etc. '00-06; 1st Secy. at London, 10; Consul-Gen. at Hankow; Sec. of Chinese Legation '16-19; Dir. Asiatic Bureau, '19.-23. Add. Peking. — p. 124 ([scan leaf 919](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n919/mode/1up))

**Yu-asa, Kurahei** Mem. House of Peers (nom. '16); b. 1874 in Fukushima; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '99; Secy. Local Gov'ts; then Sec. and Dir. of Local Administration Bureau, Home Office; prefectural Gov.; Dir. of Police Bureau, Home Office '15-16. Add. Ushigome Akagishita, Tokyo. — p. 124 ([scan leaf 919](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n919/mode/1up))

**Yuchi, Kohei** Mem. of House of Peers; b. in Miyazaki-ken, 1870; studied at Tokyo Normal Sch. and then Nippon Horitsu Gakko; passed Higher Civil Service Exam. '02; Prefectural officer for years and then transferred to the Metropolitan Police Board; was in charge of the Police of Formosa; Gov. of Fukui till '21; Dir. of the Police Affairs Bureau, Home Office, under the Seiyukai Administration. Add. Azabu Hommura, Tokyo. — p. 124 ([scan leaf 919](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n919/mode/1up))

**Yugawa, Moto-omi** Dir. of Nippon Yusen Kaisha; b. in Shizuoka; grad. Law, Imp. Univ. '93; long connected with the Com. Dep't having been its Secretary, Dir. of Maritime Bureau, etc.; Dir. of Fujita Firm, Osaka for a time; Vice-Min. of Com. 1914-16. Add. Kami-Osaki, near Tokyo. — p. 124 ([scan leaf 919](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n919/mode/1up))

**Yuhara, Gen-ichi** Dir. Tokyo High Sch. since its creation in '22; b. 1863 in Saga; first studied Medicine at Imp. Tokyo Univ., but left it without finishing in '84; held post of prof. at various Gov. Schools, Dir. of Middle Schools, Dir. Imp. Musical Academy '08-17, Dir. Tokyo Higher Girls Normal Sch. since '17. Add. Yoyogi Tomigaya, near Tokyo. — p. 124 ([scan leaf 919](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n919/mode/1up))

**Yukawa, Gen-yo, M.D. (Japanese and German)** Dir. of the Yukawa Gastro-enteric (private) Hospital, Osaka; b. 1867 in Wakayama; grad. from the Kyoto Prefectural School of Med., class '90; further at the Elective course, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '91-93, and lastly in Germany, '00-02, to study his speciality in digestive organs. Meanwhile he practised in Wakayama and served at the Nagayo Gastro-enteric Hospital; established his hospital on returning home. Add. Imabashi Sanchome, Osaka. — p. 124 ([scan leaf 919](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n919/mode/1up))

**Yukawa, Kankichi** Mng.-Dir., Sumitomo Bank; b. 1868 in Wakayama-ken; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '90; Secretary to Dept. of Communications and next to Formosan Govt.; Principal of the Post and Telegraph School; represented Japan at the International Postal Conference held at Washington, '96; Dir. of the Communication Superintending Bureau; resigned office '05 and entered Sumitomo Firm. Add. Sumitomo Bank, Osaka. — p. 124 ([scan leaf 919](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n919/mode/1up))

**Yuki, Toyotaro** Mng.-Dir. of Yasuda Holding Co. & Vice-Pres. Yasuda Bank since '22; b. in Yamagata-ken, 1877; grad. from Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '03; entering the service of the Bank of Japan was promoted to the Manager of Osaka Branch; studied banking in U.S.A., '06-09. Add. Yasuda Hozensha, Tokyo. — p. 125 ([scan leaf 920](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n920/mode/1up))

**Zumoto, Motosada** ex-M.P., Journalist, Chairman of International Press Association; b. 1862 in Tottori; grad. from Sapporo Agr. Coll. '84; was long on the staff of the Japan Mail; entered civil service for a short while and was twice appointed Per. Sec. to the late Prince Ito when he was Premier; a resident correspondent of the London Standard during the Russo-Japanese War; started the Japan Times as the only English paper edited by Japanese in '97; conducted the Seoul Press, '06-09; started in '09 the Oriental Information Agency in New York City; but returned to Tokyo in '10 to resume control of the Japan Times, '10-14; started in '16 the Herald of Asia which was suspended after the Earthquake; was attached to the Siberian Expeditionary Army '22; Made a tour to U.S.A. with Baron Shibusawa in '15 and in '21. Add. Herald Office, Kojimachi-ku Uchisaiwaicho Itchome, Tokyo. — p. 125 ([scan leaf 920](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n920/mode/1up))


## Business Directory

### Section I. Banks and Trust Cos. (For details vid. Chap. Banks and Banking)

**Aichi Bank, K.K** Tamaya-cho, Nishi-ku, Nagoya. Est. Mar., 1896. Cap. ¥15,000 (¥11,800 p.u.); res. ¥7,650; absorbed Tobi bank in '18. Pres., Y. Watanabe; Gen. Mng., M. Tajima. — p.  ([scan leaf 921](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n921/mode/1up))

**Aichiken Noko Ginko (Agricultural and Industrial Bank of Aichi Prefecture), K.K** Shinsakae-cho, Nakaku, Nagoya. Est May 1898. Cap. ¥6,000 (¥4,500 p.u.): res. ¥2,381. Pres., H. Isogai; Vice-Pres., H. Kamiya; Dirs., S. Miyata, M. Takahashi, etc. — p.  ([scan leaf 921](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n921/mode/1up))

### Section II. Brewery

**Dai Nippon Beer Brewery Co., K.K** Meguro, Tokyo. Formed March, 1906 by amalgamating Ebisu, Sapporo and Asahi breweries. Cap. ¥40,000 (¥27,000 p.u.); res. ¥16,000. The foremost Co. in this line; annual output 900,000 koku. Brands, "Ebisu," "Sapporo," "Asahi," and "Munchenner" beers. Pres. K. Makoshi; Mng.-Dir., C. Uemura, etc. — p. 131 ([scan leaf 926](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n926/mode/1up))

**Kirin Beer Brewery Co., K.K** Yamate-cho, Yokohama. Founded by some foreigners and reorganized in Feb. 1907 as K.K. with Japanese and foreign shareholders; brands, "Kirin," "Laher," "Pilsena," etc. Cap. ¥10,800 (¥8,300 p.u.); res. ¥3,664; annual output 120,000 koku. Pres., I. Itami, etc. — p. 131 ([scan leaf 926](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n926/mode/1up))

**Nichi-Ei (Anglo-Jap.) Brewery Co., K.K** Marunouchi, Tokyo. Est. June '17 as joint enterprise of Japanese and Canadian capitalists, but the latter are no longer represented. **Toyo Sugar Mfg. Co., K.K** Kagi, Formosa. Est. **Mitsukoshi Gofukuten, K.K** Suruga-cho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. A leading and the oldest dry goods store with a history of three hundred years and also pioneer department store. Reorganized as K.K. with capital ¥2,000, later to increase to ¥15,000 (¥9,000 p.u.), but reduced to ¥7,000 to liquidate Sept. damage. Branches: Osaka, Kyoto, Seoul and Dairen. Saida, etc. — p. 134 ([scan leaf 929](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n929/mode/1up))

**E. H. Hunter & Co** Hirano-cho, Kobe. Formed 1868, by E. H. Brown's Scotch Whisky, etc. Head Office, Honcho, Yokohama. Est. 1885 by late Mr. H. Isono; limited liability company May '11. Warranted purveyors to the Imperial Household and the Imperial Navy. **Mitsubishi Firm, Ltd., Gs. K** Carries on shipbuilding, Banking, Mining, Iron Works, Trading, Warehousing and Insurance business each as independent company. Iwasaki, etc. — p. 140 ([scan leaf 935](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n935/mode/1up))

**Mitsui Bussan Kaisha, K.K** General importers and exporters, Surugacho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. One of the oldest establishments in this line. Reorganized in Oct. Fukui, M. Fujise. Branches: London, New York, etc. — p. 140 ([scan leaf 935](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n935/mode/1up))

**Mitsui & Co., Gm. K** Suruga-cho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. 1893 with capital ¥60,000 in 1920. Controlling centre of all the undertakings run by the Mitsui. Pres. Baron Hachiroemon Mitsui, etc. — p. 140 ([scan leaf 935](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n935/mode/1up))

**Nichibei Silk Co., K.K** Yuraku-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. Est. Oct. 1919, with the object of exporting silk to U.S.A., etc. Capital ¥10,000 (¥2,500 p.u.) Mng.-Dir., S. Hoshino, etc. — p. 140 ([scan leaf 935](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n935/mode/1up))

**Okura-gumi, K.K** General exporters and importers, Ginza, Tokyo. The firm organized as a partnership with Baron Kihachiro Okura, well known merchant in the country, president, in 1873. Reorganized as a joint stock Co., in Dec. '11. Capital ¥10,000. In 1918 the mining and engineering dep'ts. were each formed into an independent company, the former with capital of ¥20,000 (Baron and Mr. Okuras, President and Vice-Pres. and J. Kadono (Mng.-Dir.) and the latter with capital of ¥2,000 and with T. Tokumi as managing director. — p. 140 ([scan leaf 935](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n935/mode/1up))

**Suzuka Firm** Importer, exporter and mfr. of fertilizers, reorganized as K.K. in '20; ¥50,000 fully paid up; 1-chome, Saga- cho, Fukugawa, Tokyo; connection world-wide. Pres. J. Suzuka. — p. 140 ([scan leaf 935](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n935/mode/1up))

**Suzuki & Co., unltd** Kaigandori, Kobe. Est. Mar. 1887. Capital ¥50,000 p.u. General merchants, manufacturers and shipowners with world-wide connection. **Uchida Trading Co., K.K** Naka-cho, Kobe. Est. 1917. Cap. ¥5,000 p.u; general exporters and importers. Pres. T. Yagi; Dir. S. Uchida; T. Murakami, etc. — p. 141 ([scan leaf 936](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n936/mode/1up))

**Yonei & Co** General importers and exporters, Ginza, Tokyo. ### Section XVI. Paper and Paper Wares

**Fuji Paper Mill Co., K.K** Sanjikkenbori, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Est. Nov. 1887. Cap. 77,700 (¥58,903 p.u.); res. ¥8,389; amalgamated Nippon P.M. Co. in 1911 and Asahikawa Electric Cos. in '16, Hokkaido Kogyo Co. in '19, Yokkaichi Tokyo P. Mfg. Cos. in '20, Nippon Chemical Paper-stuff Co. in '22 and Umezu and Kumano P. Mfg. Cos. in 1924; own 10 mills in Shizuoka-ken, Hokkaido and Osaka; 3 branches in China; annual product 408 million lbs. Pres. H. Okawa; Mng.-Dirs. Y. Anamizu, etc. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 942](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n942/mode/1up))

Tanaka, etc. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 942](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n942/mode/1up))

**Kyushu Paper Mill Co., K.K** Kumamoto, Kyushu. Est. in July 1903. Cap. ¥10,000 (¥4,375 p.u.); res. ¥691. Pres., H. Okawa; Mng.-Dir. T. Hasegawa, etc. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 942](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n942/mode/1up))

Kimura, E. Fujino. The Co. runs two mills, one styled Takasago Mill at Takasago, Hyogo-ken, est. 1900, and the other Nakagawa Mill in Tokyo, est. 1917, [illegible] with annual output of about 16,000 tons. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 942](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n942/mode/1up))

**Oji Paper Mill Co., K.K** General office: Kita-Toshima-gun near Tokyo. Founded in 1873. Cap. ¥65,916 (¥45,511 p.u.); res. ¥16,720. Mills at Tomakomai and 9 other places. Takashima, etc. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 942](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n942/mode/1up))

## Appendix C. Learned and Social Institutions

**Agricultural Association, Japan** Founded 1881; publishes monthly organ "Dai Nippon Nokai-ho," and maintains Tokyo Agricultural College (private) in Tokyo as its educational institution; mem. 38,000. Hon. Pres. H.I.H. Prince Nashimoto; Pres. Marq. K. Matsudaira; Vice-Pres., Count M. Hotta; Dir., Dr. S. Sawamura. Add. Akasaka Tameike, Tokyo. — p.  ([scan leaf 949](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n949/mode/1up))

**Agricultural Chemical Society of Japan, The** Founded 1924; issues Journal of the Agricultural Chemical Society of Japan, in Japanese, (abstract in European languages), monthly; mem. 1280; Pres. U. Suzuki, D. Agr. Office, Inst. of Agr. Chem., Tokyo Imp. Univ. — p.  ([scan leaf 949](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n949/mode/1up))

**America's Friends Society (Beiyu-Kyokai)** Est. 1906 by Japanese who have stayed in America and by Americans staying in Japan, with the object of promoting friendly relation between the two countries; mem. about 300. Add. 3 Shibaguchi I-chome, Tokyo. — p.  ([scan leaf 949](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n949/mode/1up))

**Anthropological Society of Tokyo, The** Est. 1884; issues The Anthropological Journal, in Japanese, monthly; mem. 450; Chief Secretary A. Matsumura, D. Sc. Office, Anth. Inst., Tokyo Imp. Univ. — p.  ([scan leaf 949](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n949/mode/1up))

**Architectural Society, Japan** Founded 1917; publishes an organ, entitled "Architecture and Society"; mem. 1,200. Pres. Y. Kataoka. Add. Dojima Bldg., Osaka. — p.  ([scan leaf 949](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n949/mode/1up))

**Architectural Society, Tokyo** Mem. 3,000; Pres., T. Sone, Dr. Eng. Office, Kojimachi Yaesucho, Tokyo. — p.  ([scan leaf 949](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n949/mode/1up))

**Asiatic Society of Japan** Pioneer association of the kind in Japan and was est. about 1880 by the late Sir Earnest Satow and others in Japan for studying and investigating Japanese customs, history, literature, etc. Meeting held monthly and proceedings published occasionally and distributed to members gratis. Pres. Dr. W. H. Solf; Vice-Pres. Rev. A. K. Reischauer & G. B. Sansom; Treasurer. N.K. Roscoe. Office. Osaka Bldg., Koj. Uchisaiwai-cho 1-chome, Tokyo. — p.  ([scan leaf 949](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n949/mode/1up))

**Association for Japanese Geographers** Est. 1923; publishes The Geographical Review of Japan, in Japanese, monthly; mem. 64. Pres. N. Yamasaki, D. Sc. Office, Geog. Inst., Tokyo Imp. Univ. — p.  ([scan leaf 949](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n949/mode/1up))

**Association of Chemical Industry, The** Est. 1817; publishes The Chemical Industry, in Japanese, monthly; mem. 862. Pres. T. Takamatsu, D. Eng. Office, 5 Higashi-dori, Marunouchi, Tokyo. — p.  ([scan leaf 949](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n949/mode/1up))

**Association of Patent Attorneys** A foundation est. April, 1922, as an advisory body in legal matters; mem. 700. Dirs., K. Inomata, S. Horie, etc. Add. Patent Bureau, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p.  ([scan leaf 949](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n949/mode/1up))

**Association of the Research on the Gastro-enterology, The** Est. 1926; publishes The Journal of Gastro-enterology, in Japanese, monthly; mem. 1270. Pres. I. Matsuo, M.D. Office, Univ. Hosp., Kyoto Imp. Univ. — p.  ([scan leaf 949](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n949/mode/1up))

**Astronomical Society of Japan, The** Est. 1908; publishes The Astronomical Herald, in Japanese, monthly; mem. 800; Chairman, K. Sotome, D. Sc. Office, Tokyo Ast. Obs., Mitaka-mura, Tokyo-fu. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 950](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n950/mode/1up))

**Aviculture Society, Japan** Established '20; associated with National Association of Audubon Society, America (Pres. Dr. T. Y. Pearson). Representatives, K. Iwabuchi and S. Washizuka. Office, Care Y. Yanagisawa, Yoyogi Sanya, Tokyo. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 950](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n950/mode/1up))

**Bankers' Association, Osaka** Founded 1897; mem. 60 banks. Add. Kitaku Nakanoshima, Osaka. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 950](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n950/mode/1up))

**Bankers' Association, Tokyo** Founded 1880; fund consists of subscriptions in proportion to the capital paid up of the associated banks; mem. 78 banks. Pres., M. Kushida. Add. Kojimachi Eirakucho, Tokyo. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 950](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n950/mode/1up))

**Barristers' Society, Japan** Founded June, 1896; issues a monthly periodical, mem. 2,700. Dirs., C. Iwata, K. Ishii, S. Uzawa, etc. Add. Kojimachi Nishi-Hibiya, Tokyo. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 950](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n950/mode/1up))

**Belgo-Japanese Association** Founded 1889; mem. about 74 comprising only those who have studied in Belgium. Secy., Mr. S. Akasaka. Add. Azabu Fujimicho, Tokyo. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 950](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n950/mode/1up))

**Botanical Society, Japan** Founded 1882; publishes a monthly Botanical Magazine. Pres. Dr. J. Matsumura; Mng.-Dir. Dr. K. Shibata. Add. Botanical Garden, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 950](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n950/mode/1up))

**British Society** Founded 1908 as a sister Society of the Japan Society of London. Mixed Japanese and British membership of about 250. Patron, H.I.H. Prince Chichibu. Honorary Secretary; Mr. A. W. Medley. Office, No. 1. Enokizaka-machi, Akasaka-ku, Tokyo. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 950](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n950/mode/1up))

**Carnegie Endowment for International Peace, Division of Economics and History, Japanese Committee** Est. in July, 191[?] in accordance with the International Conference of Economists held in 1911 in Berne. The result of investigation is published at the head office in New York, U.S.A. Pres. Baron Y. Sakatani; Secy. Y. Yamamoto. Office at Senshu Daigaku, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 950](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n950/mode/1up))

**Cellulose Institute, The** Est. 1924; publishes The Cellulose Industry, in Japanese, monthly; mem. 450. Office, Kami Fujimae-cho, Hongo-ku, Tokyo. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 950](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n950/mode/1up))

**Central Sanitation Association** Est. about 20 years ago. A semi-official organ to investigate into general sanitation affairs under the control of the Home Minister. Organized with a standing committee of 30 and a number of prominent medical and sanitation experts. Pres. Home Minister. Add. Home Office, Kojimachi-ku Otemachi, Tokyo. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 950](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n950/mode/1up))

**Central Charity Association** Created in Tokyo in 1908, at the instance of Viscounts Shibusawa and Kiyoura, and a large number of leading men either in or out of Government service. The object aimed at is to inquire into the modus operandi and condition of charity and relief works at home and abroad; to place charity bodies on a definite system and to bring them into harmonious relation: to bring charity bodies into closer touch with the general public, and to extend guidance and encouragement to charitable works and help their conduct. Chair. Viscount Shibusawa. Office, Kanda Hitotsubashi-dori, Tokyo. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 950](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n950/mode/1up))

**Ceramics Association, Japan** Founded 1892; issues a magazine; mem. 963. Pres. Visc. K. Kaneko. Add. Fukagawa Mannencho, Tokyo. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 951](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n951/mode/1up))

**Chemical and Physical Research Institute** Vid. Chapter on Education. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 951](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n951/mode/1up))

**Chemical Society of Japan, The** Est. 1878 as the Tokyo Chemical Society; changed to the present name, 1921; publishes The Journal of the Chemical Society of Japan, in Japanese, monthly; mem. 1289; Pres. Y. Shibata, D. Sc. Office, Chem. Inst., Tokyo Imp. Univ. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 951](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n951/mode/1up))

**Chikyu Gakudan (Association of Geographers & Geologists)** Est. 1924; issues "Chikyu" (The Earth), in Japanese, monthly; mem. 800; Chairman, T. Ogawa, D. Sc. Office, Geo. Inst., Kyoto Imp. Univ. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 951](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n951/mode/1up))

**Chosen Medical Society** Est. 1911; issues The Journal, in Japanese, monthly; mem. 1000; Pres. K. Shiga, M.D. Office, Gov.-Gen. Hos., Renkendo, Keijo. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 951](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n951/mode/1up))

**Civil Engineering Society** Est. 1914; issues The Journal of the Civil Engineering Society, in Japanese, bimonthly; mem. 2610 Pres. B. Kusakabe, D. Eng. Office, Yuraku-cho, Koji-machi-ku, Tokyo. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 951](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n951/mode/1up))

**Dai Nihon Butokukai (Japan Martial Virtue Ass'n.)** Founded 1898; mem. 2,167,376. Pres. Adm. R. Yashiro. Add. Heian Shrine, Kyoto. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 951](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n951/mode/1up))

**Imperial Academy** Founded in 1879 and now subject to control of Minister of Education; joined 1906 the International Association of Academies. **Invalided Soldiers' Home** Was first started in 1906 at Shibuya, a suburb of Tokyo, and was removed in June '08 to the present site at Sugamo which was presented by the Mitsui family. Only about 80 are at present living in the Home, all the sixteen thousands of invalids, being still at their own homes, partly because the pension for invalided soldiers was somewhat raised soon after the War and also because family ties are very strong in Japan. It has a fund of over ¥440,000 of which ¥100,000 was donated by Mr. I. Morimura. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 953](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n953/mode/1up))

**National Research Council** As gazetted on Nov. 26, 1920 it consists of 2 Departments, General Affairs and Scientific; has in maximum 100 scientists as its members. It comprises 8 branches of investigation, physics, chemistry, geography, biology, medicine, engineering, astronomy and geophysics, and cooperates with the International Research Council formed by the late Allied States. It will also serve as a coordinating medium for various scientific associations at home. The first general meeting was held in Dec. 1920. Pres. Dr. Baron K. Furuichi; Vice-Pres. Dr. J. Sakurai. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 958](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n958/mode/1up))

**Nippon Club** Est. 1888 by Japanese; is a non-political institution. Add. Kojimachi Yurakucho, Tokyo. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 958](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n958/mode/1up))

**Oriental Association (formerly called Taiwan Kyokai)** Founded 1898, for developing resources in Formosa, Korea and Manchuria. **Protection of Animals, Kanagawa-ken Society for** Founded in 1906 mostly by foreigners, later expanded and joined by many Japanese members, having the Prefectural Governor as President. Office, at the prefectural office, Yokohama. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 959](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n959/mode/1up))

**Protection of Animals, Kobe Society for** Founded in 1908 by Japanese and foreign residents in the port city. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 959](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n959/mode/1up))

**Protection of Animals, Tokyo Society for** Organized in 1904 under the promotion of several Unitarian ministers and members. Office, Azabu Hommuracho, Tokyo. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 959](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n959/mode/1up))

**Red Cross Society of Japan** Started in 1877, Japan having joined the Geneva Convention in 1886. The Society's hospital is reputed in accommodation and general arrangement as being the best in the Far East. It attends to training of nurses, the course of study extending over three years. Every local branch of the Society also maintains a similar service, of three year course. The latest statistics returned at the end of 1924-25 fiscal year read as follows: hospitals, 20; hospital ships, 2; medical corps, 148; doctors and pharmacists, 258; nurses and helpers, 4,484. The total membership of the society was 2,320,000 including foreigners. The in-patients at the hospitals totalled 938,647 in number of days treated, and out-patients 2,594,236. Expenditure in 1924-25 amounted to ¥51,493,511; total property assessed at ¥50,321,364. Hon. Pres., H.H. Marshal Prince Kan-in; Pres., Baron N. Hirayama. Office, Shiba Park, Tokyo. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 959](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n959/mode/1up))

**Roentgen Society of Japan, The** Est. 1923; issues Journal of the Roentgen Society of Japan, in Japanese, twice a year; mem. 410; Pres. Y. Tashiro, M.D. Office, Inst. of Ortho. Surg., Tokyo Imp. Univ. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 960](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n960/mode/1up))

**Rotary Club, Tokyo** Established 1920; social and cultural society of Japanese and foreign businessmen resident in Tokyo; membership 50. Chairman, S. Ichinomiya. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 960](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n960/mode/1up))

**Russo-Japanese Society** Founded 1906; mem. 465; Patron, H.I.H. Prince Kan-in; Pres., Visc. S. Goto. Add. Kojimachi Uchisaiwaicho, Tokyo. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 960](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n960/mode/1up))

**Seamen's Rescue Society** Gives training at the institutes in Shinagawa, Kobe and Nagasaki to 299 higher class seamen and 3,400 ordinary hands; gives employment through society's intermediation to officers and men, care for at its hospitals a number of decrepit sailors or aids families of deceased sailors. **Women's Patriotic Society** The Society owes its inception to an old woman and its present prosperity to her single-hearted devotion to the cause, Madam Okumura (d. 1907). The principal aim of the Society is to give relief to invalided soldiers and the families of those who were killed in war. The Society also looks after the schooling of orphans of soldiers killed in war. It keeps a workhouse for disabled soldiers, their families or families of those killed in war. Since 1918 the Society has attended also to relief works in general. Mem. 1,391,791; expenditure (from beginning to '23) ¥1,465,568; fund ¥2,000,213. Hon. Pres. H.H. Princess Dowager Higashi Fushimi; Chair. Mrs. Utako Shimoda. Office, Kudan, Tokyo. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 963](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n963/mode/1up))

**Young Men's Association, Japan** Founded 1922; consists of local young men's ass'ns. all over Japan. Pres. Prince F. Konoye. Add. Kojimachi Otemachi, Tokyo. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 963](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n963/mode/1up))

**Zoological Society of Japan, The** Est. 1877; publishes The Zoological Magazine, in Japanese, monthly, and Annotationes Zoologicae Japonenses, in European languages, occasionally; mem. 538; Pres. A. Oka, D. Sc. Office, Zoo. Inst., Tokyo Imp. Univ. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 963](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n963/mode/1up))

**Zootechnical Science Society of Japan** Est. 1924; issues Japanese Journal of Zootechnical Science, quarterly; mem. 350; Pres. R. Iwazumi, D. Agr. Office, Inst. Vet. Sc., Tokyo Imp. Univ. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 963](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n963/mode/1up))

**Zymurgy Society of Osaka** Est. 1910; publishes The Journal of Zymurgy, in Japanese, twice a month; mem. 4500; Pres. Y. Nishikawa. Office, Osaka Higher Tech. Sch. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 963](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n963/mode/1up))

### Medical Service

**Regulation tariff** Consultation ¥1-5. Visit ¥2-20. Prescription ¥1-2, Examination of discharge ¥1-5. Medicine, either internal or external, for one day 30 sen-¥1. Hypodermic injection ¥1-3. Serum treatment ¥3-10. Fee for operation differs according to its nature. Then the fee to a veteran physician who examines only a limited number of patients on special introduction may be not less than ¥50. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 966](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n966/mode/1up))

**What to Eat and Where to Eat** Just as in other matters of daily life foods in Japan may broadly be divided into native and European-style as to preparations, with a minor division of Chinese foods completing the whole field of operation open to the ingenuity of Japanese cooks. The native diet may again be roughly subdivided into the Tokyo and the "Kamigata" (Kyoto-Osaka) style, the latter more delicately prepared and aimed at utilizing to best advantage the inherent taste of stuffs used, while in the former which originally satisfied the less fastidious taste of the Kanto warriors of Kamakura and Yedo, sugar is more freely used, and also greater importance is attached to appearance than in the other style. It is significant to note that the "Kamigata" cooking is growing more and more popular in Tokyo and that the traditional Tokyo style is gradually losing ground. Then foreign-style restaurants are steadily acquiring greater favour, in that they are served more readily, perhaps less costly and look more up-to-date. As to expense there is not much to choose between restaurants of the two divisions, the geisha-girl fee being a conspicuous item in the bill of one and the liquor item in that of the other. We are speaking of full dress banquet. In either case one must be prepared to spend per capita ¥20, tips included. Chinese dinner is more moderate, and so is the native dinner when the professional girls' service is dispensed with. Of course the a la carte meal at chop-houses is least expensive, and it is at those Nawa-no-ren eating shops that the best of delicacies and of sake such as please dilettants are served. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 969](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1928/page/n969/mode/1up))
